# Decision Log

This log covers the conversion of the 55 Mule 3 examples in this repository into standalone `<example>-java` Spring Boot projects. `sap-data-retrieval` is `EXCLUDED — pending sapjco3.jar`. Every non-trivial decision and every deviation from a literal reading of the request has a row. Rows D-001 … D-081 were set by the migration plan; rows from D-082 on record decisions taken while the projects were written. Rows are appended and never renumbered, and a row that supersedes or amends an earlier decision names that row.

Per-project status is in [SUMMARY.md](SUMMARY.md). The source-to-target mapping, whose Decision cells cite these IDs, is in [TRACEABILITY.md](TRACEABILITY.md).

| ID | Decision | Alternatives considered | Rationale | Risks / trade-offs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-001 | 56 folders exist, so 55 are delivered; General = 35; `SUMMARY.md` has 56 rows; the eventual total is 56 | Adopt the prompt's 57/36 figures | Folder listing and tech spec 1.2.3.3 show 56; no folder can be invented | Stakeholders expecting 57 rows see 56; `SUMMARY.md` header explains |
| D-002 | Projects are root-level siblings `<folder>-java`; not added to the root reactor; originals read-only | A `java/` subfolder; a new aggregator | Matches the prompt's naming; the root `pom.xml` is excluded from change; independence | Root listing doubles in length |
| D-003 | Naming: folder case preserved in project and artifactId; package lower-cased with `_`; PascalCase main class; subpackages `model`, `endpoint`, `listener`, `scheduler` and `client` added beside the five mandated ones | Put everything in the five packages | Keeps adapters and DTOs out of `controller` and `service` | Reviewers see extra packages |
| D-004 | Standalone projects: `spring-boot-starter-parent` only; cross-cutting classes duplicated per project | A shared library module or shared parent POM | "No shared cross-project runtime", independent `mvn clean install` | Fixes to shared patterns must be applied up to 55 times |
| D-005 | Spring Boot 3.2.12 | Another 3.2.x patch | Latest 3.2 patch on Central | BOM forces Jackson 2.15.4 onto `force-wsc` (declares 2.18.4); covered by Tier 2B |
| D-006 | `spring-ws.version` = 4.1.4 overrides the BOM's 4.0.11 | Keep 4.0.11 | Prompt pins 4.1.4; a scratch build on Boot 3.2.12/Framework 6.1.15 passed | 4.1.x is built against Framework 6.2; untested corners may surface |
| D-007 | Error bodies: original literal bodies preserved; `ErrorResponse{message, upstreamSystem}` only where the original has none | One uniform JSON error for everything | The contract requires the original payloads; the prompt's "consistent shape" is honoured for new cases | Two shapes coexist: literal original bodies and `ErrorResponse` for the connector failure modes; the RAML projects send no body where the RAML declares none |
| D-008 | rest-api-with-apikit `ConflictException` → bodyless 409 (no `Content-Type`) | Reproduce whatever the runtime returned; 409 with an `ErrorResponse` body | The RAML declares 409 without a body and governs RAML projects. The original runtime status is unverified: `ConflictException` extends APIkit's `MuleRestException` and no `apikit:mapping` handles it (0.3.3) | If the diagnostic capture of 0.7.2 shows another status, clients of the original saw a different status; a new DECISIONS row and the project README then record it (0.3.3) |
| D-009 | Interactive APIkit console in the four RAML projects: `ApiConsoleController` serves `static/console/index.html` and plain-JavaScript `console.js` at `/api/console/`, the console model `api.json` that `config/RamlModel` builds from the copied RAML, and the copied RAML, schema and example files; the page lists every resource and method and has a Try It section pre-filled from the RAML examples. `ApiConsoleTest` (HtmlUnit 2.70.0) replays the README journey (0.3.2) | Drop the console; a static resource list; embed the APIkit console bundle | All four configs enable the console at `console`, and the original's Try It journey is documented [testing-apikit-with-munit/README.md:24-28]; a small committed page reproduces it without a JavaScript toolchain | The page reproduces the journey, not the APIkit console's appearance |
| D-010 | HTTP ingress on embedded Undertow with Tomcat excluded; custom reason phrases (e.g. "Invalid input data", "Not Found", "File was uploaded.") set through `ReasonPhrase` and `HttpServerExchange.setReasonPhrase` | Tomcat with the phrase dropped; a Tomcat valve | Tomcat 10.1 writes an empty reason phrase, and a scratch build confirmed that Undertow sends the custom one (0.3.3) | Relies on Undertow internals; `ReasonPhraseWireTest` guards it |
| D-011 | The seven multi-port projects of 0.3.2 get extra Undertow listeners through `AdditionalPortsConfig`, and `PortPathGuardFilter` keeps each path on its original port; every listener has its own key (0.3.5) | One port; separate apps per port | Keeps every original port and path-to-port ownership | More ports to free in tests (random ports used) |
| D-012 | Config keeps the original key names. Every credential, including the originals' demo values, is a TODO placeholder in the committed `application.yml`. The demo values are supplied by the user in the git-ignored `application-local.yml`, from the committed `application-local.example.yml`, and in-JVM test actors take theirs from `application-test.yml` | Keep the demo values in `application.yml`; blank them without a local profile | "Never hardcode credentials" governs committed main files, while the local profile keeps the original demo reproducible (0.3.5, 0.10.4) | The demo does not run until the user fills in `application-local.yml` |
| D-013 | Salesforce: OAuth2 username-password grant, then `PartnerConnection` session; consumer key/secret keys `sfdc.key`/`sfdc.secret` | SOAP `login()`; JWT bearer | The prompt names "OAuth2 username-password" matching basic auth; reuses username/password/token | Password flow blocked by default in new orgs and retired 20 Feb 2027; requester must enable it and supply connected-app credentials |
| D-014 | authenticating-salesforce-using-oauth2 keeps the web-server (authorization-code) flow | Password or JWT bearer | "Matching the original auth mechanism"; the example exists to show browser OAuth | Its IT needs a browser (Chrome) |
| D-015 | Credential keys: originals kept per project (`sfdc.user` in five, `sfdc.username` in one); NetSuite `netsuite.account` in both; Dynamics `dynamics.service-url` (original `crm.service-url`); SAP `jco.client.*` (original `sap.jco.*`) | Normalise all Salesforce keys; use original Dynamics/SAP names | Prompt: reuse original names where possible, but it lists the Dynamics/SAP/NetSuite keys explicitly | Operators must map old values to new keys (README table) |
| D-016 | NetSuite, REST only. netsuite-data-retrieval reads through the record API: a paged `q` collection query, then an instance read per id. SuiteQL is used only by get-customer-list-from-netsuite. Responses are mapped to the committed example shapes by the rules of 0.6.5, with named blockers FB-NS-01…09. `private_key_jwt` PS256 via oauth2-client. New key `netsuite.time-zone` | SuiteQL for all three searches; a SOAP client; RS256 | Record queries return ids and accept body fields only (chapter_1540810947), while the instance read returns the body fields the examples carry under documented record ids. SuiteQL table and column names for these records are unverified. SOAP is no longer available from 2028.2 (section_N3418621). The M2M guides specify PS256 (0.3.6) | N+1 requests per search, disclosed in 0.10.5. The item quantity filter reads every supply plan (FB-NS-01). A field REST does not expose is omitted, never invented, and fails the acceptance rules of 0.6.5 (FB-NS-02). The project is reported `BLOCKED — pending approval of D-016` (0.7.4, 0.10.2) until the requester approves this deviation, and stays so while any FB-NS item is open under the closure rule of 0.6.5 |
| D-017 | DSQL → SuiteQL `SELECT email, firstname, lastname FROM customer WHERE lastname LIKE ? ORDER BY lastname ASC`, with `<lastName>%` as a bound parameter | Doubling single quotes in an interpolated query; raw interpolation | Bound parameters keep user input out of the query text (section_157909186990, section_N3950559) | Requires NetSuite 2026.2 or later; table and column names unverified until the live run (FB-NS-09); the case and order semantics of `LIKE` compared with the original SOAP search cannot be observed (FB-NS-07) and are disclosed in the README |
| D-018 | Workday WWS v35.0. The published `Revenue_Management.wsdl` is fetched, its SHA-256 is checked against the pinned value, and it is then committed and used for generation (0.3.4). Username `user@tenant` | Latest v47.0 (`Submit_Customer`) | v35.0 is the newest version with `Put_Customer`, the original operation; the original commits no WSDL | The tenant may reject old versions; a hash mismatch blocks the project under D-078; a move to `Submit_Customer` would need a new decision |
| D-019 | Dynamics Dataverse Web API v9.2 `POST contacts` | Organization Service SOAP | Prompt pins Web API OData v4 | `service-url` is now the org root URL, not `Organization.svc` |
| D-020 | Connector failure modes via one `execute` wrapper; single post-reauth retry; no retry on timeout; poll-triggered flows log the classification | Global retry template | Prompt's approved additions; prevents double-submitting writes | Non-HTTP flows cannot surface statuses; logs are the evidence |
| D-021 | Tier 2A/2B test classes are disabled by class-level JUnit `ExecutionCondition`s, before any Spring context starts, when fixtures or credentials are absent; label `LIVE: NOT RUN — sandbox credentials not supplied` | Fail the build; `-DskipITs` default | `mvn clean install` must pass without prerequisites | Skips can be mistaken for passes; labels prevent it |
| D-022 | `SUMMARY.md` has separate columns per tier | One pass/fail column | Prevents Tier 1 evidence being read as parity | Wider table |
| D-023 | Conversion proceeds before fixtures exist; fixtures captured when the gate opens | Block conversion until fixtures exist | Prompt: never block Tier 1 on the license | Hand-read behaviour may diverge until fixtures run |
| D-024 | Artemis replaces ActiveMQ 5. ActiveMQ's consumer-side `RedeliveryPolicy` becomes a listener-side `RedeliveryDelayPolicy`: first redelivery after 5000 ms, later ones after 2000 ms, at most 5 redeliveries (6 deliveries), as in [jms-message-rollback-and-redelivery/src/main/app/jms-redelivery.xml:6-11] | Broker-side `redelivery-delay` only; keep the ActiveMQ 5 client | The prompt pins the Artemis starter, and Artemis applies one broker-side delay, which cannot express the 5000/2000 pair (0.3.6) | The delay is held in the consumer, so a consumer restart during the wait redelivers sooner |
| D-025 | `ee:multi-transactional` → transacted listener session + `DataSourceTransactionManager` | Narayana XA | Original is best-effort 1PC, not XA | A crash between commits behaves as the original |
| D-026 | service-orchestration XA: Narayana 3.2.0 + in-VM Artemis + Derby XA; VM `audit` → Artemis queue; VM `samsungOrder` → direct call | Drop XA | Preserves ALWAYS_BEGIN/JOIN semantics across JMS and DB | Most complex runtime in the set |
| D-027 | Docroots copied byte-identical and served at their original entry URLs. The AJAX RPC channels become `POST` endpoints, and the publish channel becomes Server-Sent Events with the `AjaxMessageDispatcher` cache semantics. A committed `mule-resource/js/mule.js` shim exposes `mule.rpc`, `mule.subscribe` and `mule.unsubscribe` (0.3.8) | Rewrite the pages to `fetch`; WebSocket/STOMP | `mule.js` shipped inside the Mule AJAX jar; a shim keeps every page file unchanged | Wire protocol changes from Bayeux to HTTP/SSE: an approval-required exception (0.10.2); both projects are reported `BLOCKED — pending approval of D-027` until it is approved (D-079) |
| D-028 | Each SOAP service serves a fixed WSDL through `SimpleWsdl11Definition`, taken from what the running original publishes. Where a committed WSDL is that contract it is served unchanged: service-orchestration `samsung.wsdl`, and xml-only `service/*.wsdl` with its served schema locations rewritten (D-069). legacy-modernization's WSDL is extracted from the SoapUI capture as `IFulfillmentService.wsdl`. The other code-first JAX-WS services get authored WSDLs with inline schemas replicating CXF's wrapped defaults: foreach `BankService` and `CreditAgencyService`, soap-webservice-security `Greeter`, and service-orchestration `IProcessOrderService.wsdl`, which replaces the stale `orders.wsdl` (D-076). JAXB types are generated from the WSDLs, except in xml-only, which has DOM endpoints, and for `IProcessOrder`, whose ported model classes define the authored schema (D-076); no standalone XSD is hand-authored | Keep JAX-WS; hand-authored XSDs | JAX-WS is forbidden, and Spring WS is contract-first | Code-first WSDLs must match CXF's wire format; `WsdlContractTest` and the SoapUI envelopes are the reference |
| D-029 | xml-only uses DOM payload endpoints on `UriEndpointMapping`, serving its three committed WSDLs and two XSDs from their original `service/` layout; only the served schema locations are rewritten (D-069). It emits `Bill ` (with its trailing space) and `initialStateEstimate` as the DW does, although `reply.xml` shows `initialStayEstimate` | Match `reply.xml` | The DW script is the behaviour; the test checks substrings only | Tier 2A decides the true bytes |
| D-030 | WS-Security ported to WSS4J 4.0.1 / OpenSAML 5.1.6; one dispatcher with URI-matched interceptors | Six dispatcher servlets | One WSDL, per-path policies as the original | SAML validator API differs from WSS4J 1.x |
| D-031 | CMIS via a hand-written AtomPub client | OpenCMIS 1.1.0 | OpenCMIS brings `cxf-rt-frontend-jaxws` | Only the one operation used is implemented |
| D-032 | SharePoint via Microsoft Graph with client credentials: get-or-create the folder, then upload with `conflictBehavior=replace` (0.3.2). Keys change as the 0.10.2 table shows | SharePoint REST with username and password | Username/password (IDCRL) sign-in to SharePoint Online is retired (0.3.6), so the original route cannot work | New credentials and an Entra app registration; an approval-required exception (0.10.2), so the project is reported `BLOCKED — pending approval of D-032` (0.7.4). The original cannot run either, so its scenarios are uncapturable in Tier 2A (0.7.2) |
| D-033 | Twitter via twitter4j 4.1.2; Stocklytics/Sentiment140 via `RestClient` | Drop the example | Keeps the original library family | The public APIs may no longer serve these calls; Tier 1 uses stubs |
| D-034 | JavaScript, Groovy, JRuby and MEL re-implemented in Java | JSR-223 engines | No DW/script runtime in scope; deterministic, testable | Numeric formatting must be matched by hand |
| D-035 | Batch jobs → plain services with explicit steps | Spring Batch | Needs no job repository; simpler 1:1 step mapping | No restartability (the original had none either) |
| D-036 | Poll watermarks persisted by `FileWatermarkStore` under `watermark.store-dir`, written only when the trigger returns normally; file triggers via `@Scheduled` | In-memory watermark; Spring Integration | Mule 3.8's default watermark store is the persistent `PartitionedPersistentObjectStore` [`WatermarkFactoryBean`, 0.3.6], so the value survived restarts | A local directory must be writable; it is git-ignored (0.4.1) |
| D-037 | Not carried into the `<example>-java` projects: the items of the 0.2.2 table. Those are the Studio metadata `mule-project.xml` and `catalog/**`; the `src/test/resources/sample_data/**` design-time previews (15 `.dwl` files, `sending-a-csv-file-through-email-using-smtp/src/test/resources/sample_data/list_csv.csv` and `xml-only-soap-webservice/src/test/resources/sample_data/Envelope.xml`); the runtime residue `querying-a-mysql-database/derby.log` and `sending-json-data-to-a-amqp-queue/work`; the stray duplicate class `filtering-a-message/src/FreeMembershipDiscountFilter.java`; the unused i18n bundles `foreach-processing-and-choice-routing/src/main/resources/messages/*.properties`; `processing-orders-with-dataweave-and-APIkit/src/main/resources/orders.xsd` and `salesforce-data-retrieval/src/main/resources/example.xml`; the unused service-orchestration test resources `service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/test/resources/{order.xsd,orderSummary.xsd,sample-request.json,sample-response.json,sample.xml,test-soapui-project.xml}` and the legacy Groovy script `legacy-modernization/src/test/resources/ShippingOrderConfirmation.groovy`; the test-scope duplicate `testing-apikit-with-munit/src/test/resources/api.raml`; the superseded `service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/main/resources/orders.wsdl` (D-076); and the four unreferenced service-orchestration classes `ItemPrice`, `ItemAsXml`, `SamsungProduct` and `OrderItemToOrderRequest`; beyond that table, the `http:request-config` `HttpRequestConfig` that no flow references [exposing-a-restful-resource-using-the-HTTP-connector/src/main/app/http-restful-resource.xml:5], which gets no `application.yml` key in exposing-a-restful-resource-using-the-HTTP-connector-java | Copy everything | None is executed or read by the original's flows, classes or tests | A reader looking for a source file in the Java project finds it listed here instead |
| D-038 | `tracking:custom-event` → structured SLF4J event with MDC | Micrometer events | Mule business events have no Spring equivalent | Monitoring dashboards differ |
| D-039 | Test infrastructure: H2 (MySQL mode), embedded Artemis, Derby, flapdoodle, UnboundID, GreenMail, MockFtpServer, Qpid Broker-J, MockWebServer | Testcontainers | No Docker dependency; mirrors the originals' embedded brokers | SQL dialect differences H2 vs MySQL; H2 therefore serves only the two projects whose SQL it runs unchanged, and the dynamic-SQL projects mock their JDBC client (D-044) |
| D-040 | IBM MQ jakarta client 9.4.5.1 | MQ JMS starter | Minimal client matching Jakarta JMS 3.1 | No embedded MQ; Tier 1 mocks the factory |
| D-041 | OAuth provider → Spring Authorization Server 1.2.7 plus a resource server. `/authorize` and `/token` stay on 8081; `ProviderLoginFilter` serves the login page and forwards the authenticated `POST`; `TokenGetToPostFilter` adapts the original `GET /token`; `LocalhostRedirectUriValidator` accepts `http://localhost*`; consent off; `client_secret_post`; tokens use `OAuth2TokenFormat.REFERENCE`, so they are opaque, and the resource server introspects them in-process against the shared `OAuth2AuthorizationService` (0.4.2) | Hand-rolled provider; JWT tokens | Standard OAuth2 semantics on the original paths and ports, with opaque tokens as the original issued | The token response's other members and lifetime are pinned only by the Tier 2A fixture |
| D-042 | Browser-driven original tests → HTTP-level tests; Selenium 4.14.1 only for `SalesforceOauthIT` | Keep PhantomJS | PhantomJS abandoned; only the Salesforce login needs a real browser | `SalesforceOauthIT` needs Chrome installed |
| D-043 | Original defects preserved: Bank5 unreachable; SAP `/salesOrder` mapping typo; currency schema type; `toXml` dropped only where no media type declares XML | Fix them | "Must not alter business logic" | Documented quirks remain |
| D-044 | `db:dynamic-query` keeps the original composition: the same SQL text, input inserted unescaped, run as a plain statement against MySQL; SOQL string composition preserved likewise | Bind the input as a parameter; refuse quote-bearing input | Binding or refusing changes the outcome of inputs containing `"` or `\`, some of which form valid SQL in the original; only the unchanged text reproduces MySQL's behaviour for every input | The injection exposure of the original is kept and disclosed in both READMEs. H2 cannot run double-quoted literals, so Tier 1 pins the SQL text with a mocked client and the engine outcomes come from Tier 2A against MySQL (0.7.2) |
| D-045 | `opportunities-response.json` treated as a lenient example and copied unchanged; `RamlExampleReader` reads it with Jackson's `JsonReadFeature.ALLOW_UNESCAPED_CONTROL_CHARS` and nothing else relaxed (0.7.1) | Fix the file; strip the raw newlines before parsing | Contract files must not change, and the raw newlines are part of the `addrText` values | Strict JSON tools reject it |
| D-046 | json-schema-validator 2.2.14 for draft-03 | networknt (draft-04+) | All nine schemas are draft-03: the eight leagues schemas and `currency-schema.json`. The three request schemas are validated at runtime; the six response schemas, `currency-schema.json` among them, are checked by `RamlContractTest` (0.6.4, 0.7.1) | Library is in maintenance mode |
| D-047 | SAP follow-on: system scope `lib/sapjco3.jar`, native lib alongside, `java.library.path`, exact supplied version recorded here | Install to a local repo | Prompt requires system scope and exact version | Platform-specific native library |
| D-048 | LICENSE/NOTICE copied per project (root LICENSE for LDAP); ported Java keeps its copyright header | Omit | CPAL attribution of derived code | none |
| D-049 | Coverage: JaCoCo LINE ≥ 0.80 on `service` and `mapper`, enforced at the `test` phase | Instruction counter; `verify` phase | The prompt ties coverage to `mvn test` | Thin services pass trivially |
| D-050 | Enforcer bans JAX-WS, CXF and Mule artefacts | Manual review | Makes the prohibition machine-checked | none |
| D-051 | EE-3.6.x and reactor-excluded examples converted identically | Skip them | All 55 are in scope | Their original tests may be stale |
| D-052 | oauth2-client-credentials has no original tests; tests are derived from its branches | none | No source assertions exist | Weakest parity evidence of the set |
| D-053 | `java.util.HashMap.toString()` reproduced in choice-exception bodies | JSON bodies | Original body is the Map's `toString` | Key order is JDK-dependent; tests assert substrings |
| D-054 | processing-orders `OrderFlow` polls `input/` and calls its own `/api/currencies` over loopback HTTP | Direct method call | The original enriches over HTTP | Needs the app's own port at runtime |
| D-055 | Fibonacci cost semantics reproduced with Spring Cache keyed on `n` | Memoised map | Cost output depends on cache hits | Cache state persists across requests, as in the original |
| D-056 | HTML templates copied byte-identical; `#[...]` tokens substituted | Thymeleaf | Byte fidelity of the pages | none |
| D-057 | MUnit suites → JUnit 5 + Mockito | none | Same assertions without the Mule runtime | none |
| D-058 | Tier 2B ITs that write records create, assert and delete their own. The read-only ITs (`SalesforceIdRetrievalIT`, `SalesforceOauthIT`, `GetCustomerListFromNetsuiteIT`) write nothing | Rely on sandbox data everywhere; add writes to the read-only examples | Prompt requirement for ITs that create data; mirrors the netsuite MUnit suite; the read-only originals create nothing | Cleanup failure leaves test data; read-only ITs and the NetSuite opportunity and item checks depend on existing sandbox data (README prerequisite) |
| D-059 | Proxies: raw byte pass-through with header scrub; SOAP proxy serves `?wsdl` from upstream with the address rewritten | Spring WS proxy | Byte fidelity (`parseResponse=false`) | Upstream demo hosts may be down |
| D-060 | legacy-modernization `<async>` file write → `@Async` executor | Synchronous write | Response returned before the file write, as in the original | Lost writes on crash, as in the original |
| D-061 | One `@RestController` per top-level RAML resource (e.g. netsuite-data-retrieval's `CustomersController`, `ItemsController`, `OpportunitiesController`). rest-api-with-apikit alone keeps a single `LeaguesController` for all its resources | One controller per RAML definition everywhere | The prompt's rule is "one per RAML resource", and it names rest-api-with-apikit's single controller as canonical, so that project is the one exception | Two conventions exist side by side |
| D-062 | Endpoint tests use only error behaviours the original exhibits; shortfalls listed in README | Invent errors to reach two | "No new behaviours" outranks the test count | Some endpoints have fewer than two error tests |
| D-063 | Production libraries for systems the prompt does not pin (versions in 0.5.1): `mysql-connector-j` + `NamedParameterJdbcTemplate`; `spring-boot-starter-data-mongodb`; `spring-boot-starter-data-ldap`; `spring-boot-starter-mail` (Jakarta Mail); `commons-net`; `spring-boot-starter-amqp`; `spring-boot-starter-oauth2-client` for Box; `commons-csv`; `jackson-dataformat-xml` | Raw drivers and protocol code; Spring Integration adapters; XStream | Boot-managed where possible, one library per protocol, no extra framework | Library behaviour differs in edge cases from the Mule transports; covered by ported assertions |
| D-064 | Flow census from an element-level parse of the 60 Mule configs: 122 flows, 21 sub-flows, 3 batch jobs and 6 batch steps. `sap-data-retrieval` holds 4 of the flows, so 118 flows, 21 sub-flows, 3 jobs and 6 steps are converted in this phase (0.1.1) | Adopt the tech spec's 99 flows / 13 sub-flows | The parse counts elements by identity; the spec's totals disagree with the files [tech spec 1.2.2.1] | The traceability identity check (0.8.2) is built on the parsed set, so a parse error would propagate |
| D-065 | `http.port` default per project: the port the original README names, else the `mule-app.properties` value, else 8081 (table in 0.3.5) | Random ports; one default for all | The originals leave `${http.port}` unset and each README tells the user which port to use | A default can collide with a local service; it is overridable |
| D-066 | Response media type follows Mule 3.8's rule: an explicit `Content-Type` wins; otherwise the payload's MIME type; nothing is sent for `*/*`, which a `set-payload` without `mimeType` produces [`HttpResponseBuilder`, `SetPayloadMessageProcessor`, 0.3.6] | Always send `text/plain` or `application/json` | Keeps the originals' headers, including their absence; Tier 1 asserts "no Content-Type" where the original sends none | Clients that sniff content may behave as they did before, not better |
| D-067 | service-orchestration's in-house `GET api/prices/<id>` on port 9999 keeps the original's 404, because the `priceService` listener accepts `POST api` only while `ProductPrice` serves `GET /prices/{productId}` [service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/main/app/fulfillment.xml:105,145; service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/se/orders/ProductPrice.java:17-23] | Make the price lookup succeed | "No new behaviour"; the 404 is inferred from the configuration and confirmed by the Tier 2A scenario `service-orchestration_inhouse-order` | If the capture shows a different result, this row records it |
| D-068 | netsuite-data-retrieval's flow `get:/oportunities` (typo) is served at the RAML path `/opportunities`; the service method keeps the flow name `getOportunities` [netsuite-data-retrieval/src/main/app/netsuite-api.xml:16,79] | Rename the method | The RAML path is the contract; method names follow flow names (0.4.2) | The misspelling survives in the method name |
| D-069 | xml-only keeps its three WSDLs and two XSDs byte-identical in the original `resources/service/` and `resources/service/xsd/` layout, including `AdmissionService.wsdl:5`'s classpath-root import `service/xsd/SOA-Message-1.0.xsd`. `WsdlQueryFilter` maps the `?xsd=` keys to classpath resources and rewrites `schemaLocation` to absolute `?xsd=` URLs in the served bytes only, as CXF served them (0.4.2) | Rewrite the committed import; move the files under `resources/wsdl/` | The contract files stay unchanged and the served URLs match the SoapUI capture. No XJC run reads them, because the endpoints are DOM payload endpoints | A tool that reads the committed `AdmissionService.wsdl` from disk cannot resolve its import, exactly as with the original; `WsdlResolutionTest` proves the served chain |

Without the key, `Undertow.start()` (2.3.17) sets `NO_REQUEST_TIMEOUT` to 60000 ms. `IDLE_TIMEOUT` applies to the channel whether or not a request is in progress. The property is a `Duration`, and a unit-less value binds as milliseconds, Mule's unit, so the key keeps the original number | Idle keep-alive connections are held for up to 50 minutes, as on the original. | D-108 | http-request-response-with-logger-java answers every HTTP method on every path on `http.port` 8084 (D-065) through the catch-all `EchoController.echo`, as the original `EchoFlow` listener on `path="/*"` with no `allowedMethods` does [http-request-response-with-logger/src/main/app/echo.xml:5]. Its `application.yml` sets `spring.mvc.dispatch-trace-request: true` and `spring.web.resources.add-mappings: false` [http-request-response-with-logger-java/src/main/resources/application.yml:21,25], and `config/DispatcherServletConfig` registers the `dispatcherServlet` bean as `AllMethodsDispatcherServlet`, a `DispatcherServlet` subclass whose `doOptions` and `doTrace` hand OPTIONS and TRACE straight to `processRequest` whenever dispatching of that method is enabled. Every response therefore carries only what `EchoController.echo` writes, the request path with no `Content-Type`: OPTIONS gets no `Allow` header, TRACE no `message/http` echo, and `GET /echo` and `TRACE /echo` both return `/echo`. With `add-mappings: false` Boot registers no static-resource handler, so none claims a path such as `/favicon.ico` | Boot's defaults: TRACE not dispatched, and Boot's own `DispatcherServlet`; `spring.mvc.dispatch-trace-request: true` with Boot's own `DispatcherServlet`, the setting of D-089 and D-102; a servlet filter that answers OPTIONS and TRACE itself; leaving OPTIONS and TRACE to the container | The original listener sends every method to `EchoFlow`, whose reply is the request path [http-request-response-with-logger/src/main/app/echo.xml:5-6]. In Spring Framework 6.1.15, `FrameworkServlet.doOptions` adds an `Allow` header after the handler unless the handler set one, and `FrameworkServlet.doTrace` falls through to `HttpServlet.doTrace`, which writes a `message/http` echo of the request, unless TRACE is dispatched and the handler set the response type `message/http`. A scratch run on Boot 3.2.12 and Undertow 2.3.17, with a catch-all handler for every method that writes the request path and no `Content-Type`, gave four results. With the key `true` and Boot's `DispatcherServlet`, `TRACE /echo` sent `Content-Type: message/http` and the echo after `/echo`. With the subclass and the key `false`, TRACE again answered with the `message/http` echo. With the subclass and the key `true`, OPTIONS, TRACE and GET each answered 200 with body `/echo`, no `Content-Type` and no `Allow`. A filter would answer OPTIONS and TRACE outside `EchoController.echo`, and leaving them to the container keeps the `Allow` header and the echo. `FrameworkServlet`'s two dispatch flags are private and have no getters, so the subclass keeps its own copies through the overridden setters | `AllMethodsDispatcherServlet` relies on `FrameworkServlet` internals (`doOptions`, `doTrace`, `processRequest`), so a Spring Framework upgrade that changes them needs the class revisited. The project's `dispatcherServlet` bean replaces the one `DispatcherServletAutoConfiguration` creates, so each `spring.mvc` setting that auto-configuration applies to the servlet, the two dispatch flags among them, takes effect only where `DispatcherServletConfig` applies it. TRACE is answered with the path like any other method, unlike the 405 that the POST-only listeners of sending-json-data-to-a-jms-queue-java and adding-a-new-customer-to-workday-revenue-management-java give it (D-089, D-102) |

Boot registers no `FormContentFilter`, no `StandardServletMultipartResolver`, no `MultipartConfigElement`, no static-resource handler, no `BasicErrorController` and no `/error` error page. `SoapProxyController.main` therefore reads the request body bytes unchanged for every method and Content-Type, `multipart/related` (MTOM) and `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` included, and `SoapProxyService` forwards them (D-059); every path other than `/`, `/error` and `/favicon.ico` included, raises `NoHandlerFoundException` or `NoResourceFoundException`, which `GlobalExceptionHandler.noListener` answers with 404 `No listener for endpoint: <path>` | Keep Boot's defaults; disable only multipart parsing; keep `ErrorMvcAutoConfiguration` and map `/error` to the 404 in a controller | The original listener at `path="/"` sets no `allowedMethods` and hands the request to `cxf:proxy-service payload="envelope"`, which forwards the envelope as received [proxying-a-soap-api/src/main/app/soap-api-proxy.xml:6-7]; Mule serves no `/error` endpoint, so `/error` matched no listener like any other path. Under Boot's defaults, `PUT`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` bodies arrived with 0 of 7 bytes, a `multipart/form-data` body with 0 bytes, a `multipart/related` body answered 500 before the handler ran, and `GET /error` answered 500 with Boot's JSON body (`"status":999`). With the five settings every body arrived intact (7, 202 and 77 bytes) and `GET /error` answered 404 `No listener for endpoint: /error`. | D-113 | get-customer-list-from-netsuite-java's `CustomerHtmlMapper.toRows` (DW-04) [get-customer-list-from-netsuite/src/main/app/get-customer-list-from-netsuite.xml:13-32] writes the three cells of a row by hand as text lines: a `null` or absent `lastname`, `firstname` or `email` value becomes `<td/>`, the empty string becomes `<td></td>`, and any other value becomes `<td>` + its text + `</td>` with `&`, `<` and `>` replaced by `&amp;`, `&lt;` and `&gt;` and every other character, quotes included, unchanged. A `null` element of the list renders as a row of three `<td/>` cells. The first line is `<?xml version='1.0' encoding='UTF-8'?>`, while the committed `get-customer-list-from-netsuite/src/test/resources/example.xml:1` declares `encoding='windows-1250'` [get-customer-list-from-netsuite-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/get_customer_list_from_netsuite/mapper/CustomerHtmlMapper.java] | Write every missing or empty value the same way (`<td/>` or `<td></td>`); omit the cell; also escape `"` and `'`; copy the `windows-1250` declaration; serialise with a StAX or DOM writer; reject a `null` list element | The DW 1.0 XML writer skips no null value unless `skipNullOn` is set, and DW-04 sets none; a null value is written as an inline-closed empty element and a string, the empty one included, between start and end tags (0.6.1: null handling follows the DW 1.0 writer). XML text content requires `&` and `<` to be escaped and allows `>` to be; quotes need escaping only inside attribute values, and DW-04 emits no attribute. In DW 1.0 a field selector such as `$.lastName` on a null element yields null, which the writer renders as an empty element. The declaration names the encoding of the written text, which is UTF-8 in this application; `example.xml` carries the encoding of the machine that produced it. `CustomerPageRenderer.render` (SC-09) drops the declaration line, so the page bytes do not depend on it. Hand-written lines pin the two-space indentation, `\n` separators and single-quoted declaration of `example.xml` byte for byte, which library serialisers format in their own way | The null and empty-string forms follow the DW 1.0 writer's documented defaults, and the `>` escape is not documented; none of the three is yet observed on the original; the Tier 2A `customers-found` and `no-customers` captures confirm them, and any difference is recorded in a new row. Values holding `]]>` or XML-illegal control characters are written as described, which the original's writer may render differently |

A `NullPointerException` subtype that holds the map keeps both facts in the thrown object: `@ExceptionHandler(NullPointerException.class)` selects the branch by type, `instanceof NullPointerException` stays true as `causedBy` requires, and the body needs no request state and no second parse of the body bytes. `NullPointerException` declares only `()` and `(String)` constructors, so the cause is attached with `initCause` | The map is held by reference, not copied; `ValidationService` does not modify it after the throw. | D-131 | web-service-consumer-java carries the literals of `ws:consumer-config` `Web_Service_Consumer` under the prefix `tshirt.*`, named after the `TshirtService` service element the config names [web-service-consumer/src/main/app/tshirt-service-consumer.xml:3; web-service-consumer-java/src/main/resources/wsdl/tshirt.wsdl:226]: `tshirt.service-name` `TshirtService`, `tshirt.port-name` `TshirtServicePort` and `tshirt.service-address` `http://tshirt-service.cloudhub.io`. `tshirt.api-key`, the `apiKey` of the flow's DataWeave `soap.header` [web-service-consumer/src/main/app/tshirt-service-consumer.xml:17-23], shares the prefix (D-012). The prefix also holds `tshirt.connect-timeout` `30000` and `tshirt.response-timeout` `10000` milliseconds, which bound the connection attempt and the response wait of each call to the service [web-service-consumer-java/src/main/resources/application.yml:16-23]. The original sets no timeout, so both values are the Mule 3.8 implicit defaults its calls ran with: the TCP client socket's 30000 ms connection timeout [`DefaultHttpRequesterConfig.java:59`; `DefaultTcpClientSocketProperties.java:17,22`] and the 10000 ms `defaultResponseTimeout` [`DefaultMuleConfiguration.java:99`]. Neither key is among the five keys 0.3.5 lists as added by the Java design. `wsdlLocation` `tshirt.wsdl` has no key: the copied `src/main/resources/wsdl/tshirt.wsdl` is the input of the `xjc-tshirt` execution [web-service-consumer-java/pom.xml:118-125] | A prefix named after the config element (`web-service-consumer.*` or `ws.consumer-config.web-service-consumer.*`); 10000 ms for both phases, so that one value bounds the connection attempt and the response wait; no timeout keys, leaving both to the message sender's defaults (Spring WS 4.1.4 `HttpUrlConnectionMessageSender`: 60 s connect, 60 s read); no connect timeout (`HttpURLConnection`'s default 0, an infinite timeout) | 0.3.5 names the key of a non-secret literal after its element. The API key belongs to the flow's SOAP header, not to `ws:consumer-config`, so a prefix named after the config element would hold a key the config does not carry, while every key of the prefix concerns the `TshirtService` service. The original sets neither `connectorConfig` nor any timeout, so its calls ran on Mule 3.8's default HTTP requester: `WSConsumerConfig` selects the HTTP module for an `http` address without `connectorConfig` or `connector-ref` [mulesoft/mule `mule-3.8.0` `WSConsumerConfig.java:77-96,116-143`], and `HttpRequesterBuilder` gives it a plain `DefaultHttpRequesterConfig` [`HttpRequesterBuilder.java:133-143`]. An unset response timeout resolves to the event timeout, `defaultResponseTimeout` 10000 ms [`DefaultHttpRequester.java:386-395`; `DefaultMuleEvent.java:789-798`; `DefaultMuleConfiguration.java:99`], and the TCP client socket's connection timeout is 30000 ms [`DefaultHttpRequesterConfig.java:59`; `DefaultTcpClientSocketProperties.java:17,22`]. Timeouts keep their original values (0.10.5), so `tshirt.response-timeout` keeps the 10000 ms response bound and `tshirt.connect-timeout` the 30000 ms connection bound the original applied. The message sender's 60 s defaults would let a call wait six times as long for a response and twice as long for a connection; a single 10000 ms value would fail a connection that takes between 10 s and 30 s, which succeeded on the original; and without a connect timeout an unanswered connection attempt would never end | Mule sets the response timeout as the request timeout of its async HTTP client and waits for the response without a limit of its own [`GrizzlyHttpClient.java:238-243,428`]; a client that applies `tshirt.response-timeout` as a socket read timeout bounds each read instead, so a response that keeps arriving in parts can take longer than 10 s. Neither value comes from a measured latency of `tshirt-service.cloudhub.io`. The two timeout keys have no Mule property, so a user of the original finds no counterpart for them in its configuration |
| D-132 | `tools/traceability_check.py` (D-075) reads the `## <example>-java` section of `TRACEABILITY.md`, its heading optionally in backticks, up to the next `# ` or `## ` heading, with this cell grammar [tools/traceability_check.py:1537-1555]. A line holding a pipe that is followed by a delimiter row starts a table, whose body runs over every following non-blank line holding a pipe, as GFM renders it, so a table written directly under another without a blank line joins it; a table is the forward, backward or generated-types table when its header, lower-cased with whitespace collapsed, equals that table's 0.8.2 column names, and the bodies of all tables with that header are read as one [tools/traceability_check.py:47-49; tools/traceability_check.py:1463-1465; tools/traceability_check.py:1491-1534]. Cells split on unescaped pipes; a backslash-escaped pipe is a literal pipe; backticks and surrounding `**` and `__` markers are removed [tools/traceability_check.py:81; tools/traceability_check.py:1443-1460]. The Target, Test, backward-link, Generated package and Replaces source cells split on `<br>`, `;` and `,`, and Generated classes also on whitespace; `n/a`, `none`, `—` and `-`, in any letter case, are no entry; a Target or Test entry ending in `()` is read without it, while backward identities are compared as written [tools/traceability_check.py:60; tools/traceability_check.py:79-80; tools/traceability_check.py:1468-1480; tools/traceability_check.py:2419-2455]. The Decision cell is read whole, as D-146 sets, and the `WSDL or XSD` cell as D-147 sets. The line of an element in an identity or a locator is the line of its start tag's `<` in the raw file, found by aligning the k-th start tag with the k-th parsed element [tools/traceability_check.py:682-732]. The element part of a strategy identity is the bare local name in the Mule core namespace and the file's own `prefix:local` otherwise (`#choice-exception-strategy:15`, `#apikit:mapping-exception-strategy:18`); flows and batch elements are `#flow:`, `#sub-flow:`, `#batch:job:` and `#batch:step:` plus the `name` attribute [tools/traceability_check.py:664-667; tools/traceability_check.py:2238-2251]. The example's `src/main/app/mule-deploy.properties` path is a source identity, the backward link of the main class (0.8.2) [tools/traceability_check.py:2238]. A locator is `<path>:<line>` or `<path>:<line>-<line>`, the path repository-relative under `<example>/`, and its start line is the one resolved; a DataWeave or script row is `DW-nn <locator>` or `SC-nn <locator>`, whose ID, path, first line and written last line D-149 holds to the transform inventory; any Source identity cell may end with a `[<locator>; ...]` group [tools/traceability_check.py:71-74; tools/traceability_check.py:77; tools/traceability_check.py:2419-2440; tools/traceability_check.py:3559-3580]. A Target or Test entry is `<FQCN>#<method>`, a class, or a `src/test/capture/` path, which may also be written with an `<example>-java/` prefix, as may a capture path in the backward table; a nested class is named by its binary name with `$` (`Outer$Inner`), which for a top-level class is its FQCN [tools/traceability_check.py:2408-2416; tools/traceability_check.py:2443; tools/traceability_check.py:3860-3886]. A backward link is a forward identity, a bare `DW-nn` or `SC-nn` that stands for its ID row, or a `D-nnn` row of `DECISIONS.md`, whose IDs are the first cells of its table rows that read `D-` and three or more digits [tools/traceability_check.py:75; tools/traceability_check.py:78; tools/traceability_check.py:1558-1571; tools/traceability_check.py:3958-3966]. This row sets syntax only: 0.8.2's identity forms and its exact equality of identity and edge sets are unchanged, and D-149 and D-151 set which identities, anchors and locators are required or accepted | Read only the literal 0.8.2 forms, one name per cell, with no `()` tolerance and Markdown emphasis kept; start a table body only after a blank line; count an element's line from its first attribute or its end tag; write strategy element names with a fixed prefix or none; name nested classes by their canonical name `Outer.Inner` | 0.8.2 fixes the identity forms and the three tables but not how a Markdown cell holds several names, how an element's line is counted or how a nested class is written. `TRACEABILITY.md` is read as GFM renders it, so the parser takes what renders as one table, and `;`, `,` and `<br>` are separators a cell can hold without breaking its row. `ElementTree` reports no line numbers, and the start-tag line is where the 0.6.2 citations place an element: the strategy of `implementing-a-choice-exception-strategy/src/main/app/choice-error-handling.xml:15-47` is the 0.8.2 example `#choice-exception-strategy:15`. The file's own prefix keeps the identity equal to the source text. The `$` binary name is what the class files and `javap` carry, so an entry resolves without mapping `.` back to `$`, which is ambiguous where a package and a class share a name | A table placed directly under another without a blank line joins it and is read under the first header; an entry that itself holds `;`, `,` or, in Generated classes, whitespace cannot be written; the end of a `<line>-<line>` range in a locator group is not checked, unlike the last line of an ID row (D-149); a start tag split over several lines is known by its first line only; an author who writes `Outer.Inner` gets an unresolved entry; two configurations that bind one namespace to different prefixes yield differently spelled identities |
| D-133 | The handwritten class and public-method set of 0.8.2 is read from `javap -p -v` (D-135) of the class files under `target/classes` and `target/test-classes` with these membership rules. Not handwritten: `module-info.class`; anonymous and local classes, whose binary name has a `$` segment that starts with a digit (`A$1`, `A$1L`); and each class file whose top-level class was compiled from a `target/generated-sources/jaxb` source, which belongs to the generated set [tools/traceability_check.py:2792-2794; tools/traceability_check.py:2806-2809; tools/traceability_check.py:2861-2885]. Not methods: constructors, the static initialiser `static {}`, fields, and members flagged `ACC_SYNTHETIC` or `ACC_BRIDGE`, such as lambda bodies and generic bridge methods [tools/traceability_check.py:2688-2697]. Every other method exists for the edge check. A method is public when it carries `ACC_PUBLIC`, and each public method is required as a backward row except the implicit enum and record members D-144 identifies by their compiled form; a method identity is `<class>#<name>` with no descriptor, so overloads share one identity [tools/traceability_check.py:2697; tools/traceability_check.py:2707-2712]. A class compiled into both trees is one class with the union of its methods [tools/traceability_check.py:2886-2893]. Besides each handwritten class and each `<class>#<public method>`, the required backward set holds every handwritten `<class>#<method>` a Target or Test cell names, public or not, every generated class or method a Target or Test cell names (D-145), and the path of each `src/test/capture/**/*.java` file [tools/traceability_check.py:3934-3948]. A scratch class with two constructors, a static initialiser, a private method, a lambda, a bridge `compareTo`, the overloads `over()` and `over(int)`, an anonymous class, a local class and a public nested class gave the required set `p.A`, `p.A#anon`, `p.A#compareTo`, `p.A#lam`, `p.A#local`, `p.A#over`, `p.A$Inner`, `p.A$Inner#m`, and javap was given 2 of its 4 class files, on the script as amended by D-144 and D-145 as well. This row sets no name-based exclusion; 0.8.2's identity forms and its exact equality of the backward set are unchanged | List every class file and member javap prints; exclude compiler members by name patterns such as `lambda$`, `access$` or `<init>`; key methods by name and descriptor; read the handwritten set from the `src/main/java` and `src/test/java` sources instead of the compiled output; require backward rows only for public methods even where a forward cell names a non-public one | 0.8.2 asks for one backward row per handwritten class and public method listed by `javap -p`. Constructors, static initialisers, synthetic and bridge members, and anonymous and local classes are emitted by the compiler with no declaration a backward row could name in the `<FQCN>#<method>` form; the binary names of anonymous and local classes are `Outer$<digits>` and `Outer$<digits><Name>` (JLS 13.1), and the access flags of `javap -v` mark synthetic and bridge members without guessing from names. `<FQCN>#<method>` carries no parameter types, so the matrix cannot tell overloads apart. Reciprocity (0.8.2) needs every forward edge inverted in the backward table, so a non-public method a cell names needs its backward row. Generated classes are checked against the generated-types table instead (0.8.2) | Code inside an anonymous or local class or a lambda has no backward row of its own and is traced only through its enclosing method; overloads with different behaviour share one row; a class its author names with a `$` followed by a digit is skipped; a class compiled into both trees under one name gets one row |
| D-134 | `tools/traceability_check.py` exits 0 only when it printed no failure line and every figure of the result line is complete; 1 for any failure line or incomplete figure; 2 for a usage, environment or parse error, printed as one `error: ...` line on stderr; and 3 when any line of the section holds `EXCLUDED — pending sapjco3.jar`, checked before the original's source is parsed and before any dump is written, printed on stdout with `TRACEABILITY.md` not written [tools/traceability_check.py:574-579; tools/traceability_check.py:3425-3427; tools/traceability_check.py:4783-4793; tools/traceability_check.py:4874-4875; tools/traceability_check.py:4907-4923]. Input it cannot read unambiguously exits 2 instead of being counted: a table row with more cells than its header; a `flow`, `sub-flow`, `batch:job` or `batch:step` without `name`; an `munit:test` without `name`; an XML parse error or a start tag that does not align with its parsed element; a RAML line or file outside the subset D-150 reads, an unbalanced flow collection included; a javap block without `this_class`, member list, `descriptor` or `flags`, or a javap batch that lists a different number of classes than it was given; a `## <example>-java` heading present twice; and invalid UTF-8 in `TRACEABILITY.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `SCENARIOS.txt` or `mule-deploy.properties` [tools/traceability_check.py:587-593; tools/traceability_check.py:692-718; tools/traceability_check.py:1527-1532; tools/traceability_check.py:1545-1548; tools/traceability_check.py:1645-1875; tools/traceability_check.py:1977-1982; tools/traceability_check.py:2219-2223; tools/traceability_check.py:2288-2289; tools/traceability_check.py:2303; tools/traceability_check.py:2644-2653; tools/traceability_check.py:2684-2687; tools/traceability_check.py:2777-2782]. D-137, D-138, D-147, D-148, D-149 and D-151 add exit-2 cases of their own: a result line that cannot be locked or recorded or a section changed during the check, a refused argument or `--dump-sets` destination, unreadable jaxb2 inputs, a Tier 1 test source or `@DisplayName` the scan cannot read, an inventory that lacks the example or disagrees with the parsed source, and a missing `SCENARIOS.txt`. Containment is tested both by path and by directory device and inode. FILE is also refused when it is an existing directory or a file with more than one hard link. These checks run again at each of the two dump writes, and each write goes to a descriptor whose file was checked. Where `os.open` accepts `dir_fd` and `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_DIRECTORY` exist, the resolved parent folder is opened first. It is refused unless the destination resolves to the same path again, the parent path still has the opened folder's device and inode, and the folder lies outside both repositories by path and by the `..` chain of its descriptor. The file is then opened by name relative to that folder with `O_CREAT` and `O_NOFOLLOW` and without `O_TRUNC`. Elsewhere the resolved path is opened without truncation and must still resolve to the opened file and lie outside both repositories. The opened file must be a regular file with one link before it is truncated, so `/dev/null`, FIFOs and devices are refused. Each write also lists the device and inode of every folder and regular file in both repositories; the receiving folder is refused on a match before the file is opened, and the opened file before it is truncated, which covers bind mounts of a repository subdirectory or file. The partial and full dumps keep their content [tools/traceability_check.py:4165-4478]. An external file that is legitimately hard-linked is refused. A folder name holding `:` or surrounding whitespace is refused; none of the 56 original folder names has one. The device-and-inode listing walks both repositories, `target/` and `.git` included, on each of the two dump writes, about 6 µs per entry; a folder or file it cannot list is not covered, and a folder moved into a repository after the listing is caught only by the path and `..` checks. Without `dir_fd`, `O_NOFOLLOW` or `O_DIRECTORY` (Windows) the checks are path-based, and a parent folder swapped concurrently can leave a new empty file inside a repository before the refusal. Without `O_PATH` (macOS, BSD) every ancestor folder of FILE must be readable. A FIFO without a reader ends with `cannot write` instead of a refusal |

A method `@DisplayName` whose argument is not one string literal (`"…"` or `value = "…"`), a `@DisplayName` on no method or type, two `@DisplayName`s on one declaration, an unterminated literal, an unbalanced brace or parenthesis, or a `@Retention` argument that is not one `java.lang.annotation.RetentionPolicy` constant, or a second `@Retention`, on an `@interface` that the annotations of a labelled method or of its enclosing types reach and whose meta-annotations, through any chain of Tier 1 `@interface`s, carry a Jupiter testable annotation, `@Disabled` or `@Nested` stops the check with exit 2 and one line naming `<path>:<line>`. The previous scan counted a helper such as `@DisplayName("hello-world_hello") private void helper() {}`, and an argument such as `@Tag("a{b;c)")` ended its declaration early. The counted set follows JUnit Jupiter 5.10.5, the Boot 3.2.12 BOM version: `IsTestableMethod` rejects static, private and abstract methods and requires `void` for `@Test` and `@TestTemplate` and a value for `@TestFactory`; `IsNestedTestClass` accepts only non-static member classes annotated `@Nested`; `@Disabled` methods and classes are skipped; and Surefire's default exclude `**/*$*` leaves static nested classes unselected. A 5.10.5 launcher run over a case set covering each of these rules executed exactly the methods the scan counts. Simple-name matching accepts JUnit 4's `@Test`, which the Jupiter engine never runs. `javap -v` resolves names exactly but carries neither the source path that the `it`, `capture` and `*IT.java` exclusions use nor the annotation line the diagnostics name. A constant or text-block argument has no value without compiling or reimplementing Java constant folding, so such an argument fails closed. Jupiter reads annotations through `AnnotationSupport.findAnnotation`, which sees runtime-visible annotations only: an `@interface` without `@Retention` has CLASS retention (JLS 9.6.4.2), and a SOURCE or CLASS `@interface` is absent at run time together with its meta-annotations. Following every `@interface` counted a method under `@Test @interface Scenario {}`, which a 5.10.5 launcher run does not discover; the same run over default, SOURCE, CLASS and RUNTIME retention, a chain with one CLASS link, a static-import `RUNTIME` and a CLASS-retained composed `@Disabled` executed exactly the methods the scan counts. javac accepts a parenthesised enum constant as the `@Retention` argument, so parentheses are read | Conditional annotations such as `@EnabledOnOs` or `@DisabledIf` take effect only at run time and are not read, so a conditionally skipped method still counts. A `@Retention` argument form javac accepts beyond those read here stops the check (exit 2) where it decides a count, until it is written as `RetentionPolicy.RUNTIME`. The plan is not part of the repository, so the script carries the inventory itself and stays a single standard-library file (D-075). One identity per line follows 0.6.1, under which `TRACEABILITY.md` maps each line identity to the row's target, and the leagues row is the one row 0.6.1 traces per resource-method through 0.6.4. The 0.6.1 rows place each construct in a configuration of the example, so a locator to any other line of a configuration names nothing the inventory or the element parse knows. The consistency check fails closed when an original drifts from the inventory, and sap-data-retrieval's setters, which have no ID, would otherwise turn its exit 3 into exit 2 | The inventory duplicates 0.6.1: a plan revision needs the constants updated by hand, and the consistency check detects drift of the originals, not of the plan. The token rule reads line numbers out of prose cells, so a cell that names a line in another sense would add an identity. The SAP follow-on must add its six DW IDs before its section can drop the EXCLUDED marker. A locator to an attribute-continuation line of a configuration no longer resolves unless it is an inventory line. An excluded example's source parse errors are not reported |
| D-150 | `tools/traceability_check.py` (D-075) reads each original RAML file through `parse_raml` with a fail-closed subset of RAML 0.8. Line 1, after an optional byte-order mark, is `#%RAML 0.8`. Every other nonblank, non-comment line outside a block scalar or flow collection is a `key:` line, a `-` sequence entry, or a further line of a plain scalar indented deeper than the key or entry that starts it. The keys of the root, of each resource, of each method and of each `responses` mapping align with their siblings and are a RAML 0.8 property, a lowercase method, a `/` resource path or a three-digit status code. The root properties are `title`, `version`, `baseUri`, `baseUriParameters`, `uriParameters`, `protocols`, `mediaType`, `schemas`, `securitySchemes`, `securedBy`, `documentation`, `resourceTypes` and `traits`. The resource properties are `displayName`, `description`, `type`, `is`, `securedBy`, `uriParameters` and `baseUriParameters`. The method properties are `description`, `headers`, `protocols`, `queryParameters`, `body`, `responses`, `is`, `securedBy` and `baseUriParameters`. A resource, method or `responses` key takes an indented mapping and no inline value other than a YAML null (`~`, `null`), and a `/` key stands only in the root or a resource mapping. Below these four mappings every key or sequence entry aligns with its siblings and fits its parent: a status code holds only `description`, `headers` and `body`; a body holds media types (keys containing `/`) and `schema`, `example` and `formParameters`, and a media type holds only those three; `headers`, `queryParameters`, `uriParameters`, `baseUriParameters` and `formParameters` hold named parameters, and a named parameter holds only the RAML 0.8 parameter properties `displayName`, `description`, `type`, `enum`, `pattern`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `minimum`, `maximum`, `example`, `repeat`, `required` and `default`; `enum` holds scalar sequence entries; the scalar properties (root `title`, `version`, `baseUri` and `mediaType`, `displayName`, `description`, `schema`, `example` and the parameter properties other than `enum`) hold no indented key or sequence entry. The content of root `schemas`, `traits`, `resourceTypes`, `securitySchemes`, `documentation`, `protocols` and `securedBy`, of resource `type`, `is` and `securedBy`, and of method `is`, `securedBy` and `protocols` is checked only for the line forms above and the `/` key rule. A plain-scalar continuation line whose whole content is a lowercase method, a three-digit status code or a `/` token fails. A file that declares no resource-method fails. Each violation exits 2 with the single line `error: <raml path>:<line>: <reason>`, or `error: <raml path>: declares no resource-method` | Skip every line the key pattern does not match (the script's first version); a complete YAML parser written into the script; also accept a scalar on the line after its key, quoted scalars spanning lines, and resource, method or `responses` bodies written as flow mappings or `!include`; accept any first line; accept a RAML file that declares no resource-method; check only the root, resource, method and `responses` keys and read the lines below them by indentation only (this row's previous version); read a continuation line that is only a method, status code or `/` token as text; hold the content of `traits`, `resourceTypes`, `securitySchemes`, `schemas` and `documentation` to the method, body and parameter lists | 0.8.2 adds "the RAML resource-method pairs" to the source set, which must equal the forward table's identity column, and D-075 makes a source that cannot be parsed exit 2. When unmatched lines were skipped, a resource or method written without its colon (`/items`, `get`), a method key in the wrong case or at the wrong indentation, or a misspelt `responses` key dropped its identities or statuses with no error, and a file that was not RAML left a RAML-backed project with no methods. Python's standard library has no YAML parser and the script is one standard-library file (D-075). The subset covers every construct of the five original RAML files, `netsuite-api.raml`, `currency.raml`, `leagues.raml`, `sap-api.raml` and `api.raml`: a scratch run gave the same 20 resource-methods and statuses before and after the change. A construct outside the subset stops the run at its line instead of being misread. Sequence entries and continued plain scalars are the YAML forms RAML 0.8 files use for `documentation`, `protocols`, `is`, `securedBy` and long descriptions. The property lists are those of the RAML 0.8 specification, so any other key at these four levels is misspelt or misplaced. With the lines below these four levels unchecked, a method placed under `displayName`, a status placed under another status, or a `post` written without its colon on the line after a `description` value dropped an identity or a status with no error, and the full check of a RAML-backed project still passed. The status, body, media-type and named-parameter property lists are also those of the RAML 0.8 specification, and a scalar property has no keys of its own, so a key or entry under it is misplaced. Trait, resource-type and security-scheme bodies legitimately hold method-like keys such as `get?`, and documentation and schemas hold free text, so their content is not held to the lists. A continuation line that is only a method, status code or `/` token reads equally as a key missing its colon, so it stops the run instead of being taken as text. A RAML file declares an API, so a file with no resource-method has nothing for the matrix to trace | Valid RAML 0.8 that uses a form outside the subset exits 2 until `parse_raml` reads it: a scalar on the line after its key, a quoted scalar spanning lines, a flow-mapping or `!include` body, or a property missing from the lists. None of the five originals uses such a form. A RAML fragment, such as a trait or resource-type file, placed under an original's `src/main/api` or `src/main/resources` also exits 2. Valid RAML 0.8 that writes a named parameter as a sequence of alternative definitions also exits 2, and so does a description whose continuation line is only a method name, a status code or a `/` path until it is reworded or written as a block scalar. Inside root `schemas`, `traits`, `resourceTypes`, `securitySchemes`, `documentation`, `protocols` and `securedBy`, resource `type`, `is` and `securedBy`, and method `is`, `securedBy` and `protocols`, lines are checked only for their line form and for `/` keys, so another YAML error there is not reported |
| D-151 | `tools/traceability_check.py` (D-075) embeds the 0.6.3 scenario inventory and a branch-outcome map as data. `SCENARIO_INVENTORY` holds the complete scenario suffix list of each of the 51 non-RAML-backed examples; `RAML_SCENARIO_INVENTORY` holds the suffixes 0.6.3 names beside the mechanical RAML set of the four RAML-backed examples (rest-api-with-apikit `post-teams-409-conflict`; processing-orders-with-dataweave-and-APIkit `order-file`, `order-file-failure`; netsuite-data-retrieval `name-with-quote-refused`, `title-with-quote-refused`, `items-multi-line-plan`, `collection-paging-two-pages`; testing-apikit-with-munit none); sap-data-retrieval has no entry. With the file as the only source, a missing `SCENARIOS.txt` dropped every scenario identity and display-name check, and removing `content-based-routing_favicon-filtered` from the file and the matrix left the favicon filter's rejection unrequired while the MEL row `content-based-routing.xml:7` still bound the filter. An embedded list makes the expected set independent of the delivered evidence, and comparing the file with it keeps the Tier 2A gate list (0.7.2) complete; 0.4.1 places the file in the 51 non-RAML-backed projects and in none of the four RAML-backed ones. The plan is not part of the repository, so the script carries the inventory and stays one standard-library file (D-075, as in D-149). Naming the scenario in the binding ties each outcome to the test of that scenario, and a `choice` has no outcome of its own beyond its branches. The traversal rule also lists scenarios that reach a branch through another flow, such as `admit-patient` on the `when` branches of the xml-only-soap-webservice mocks. 0.6.3 states "one scenario per missing parameter" and names `missing-name` … `missing-term`, and the delivered dataweave-with-flowreflookup file writes its regions without their group label | The inventories duplicate 0.6.3: a plan revision needs the constants updated by hand, and the consistency check detects drift of the originals, not of the plan. The 32 lists not yet on disk follow 0.6.3 as read here, so a `SCENARIOS.txt` delivered in another form (for example `incomplete-request-missing-name`) fails until the file or the inventory changes. Scenarios whose branch the original leaves open, such as `order-request`, `order-proxy` and `order-error` of service-orchestration-and-choice-routing, `client-error` of soap-webservice-security and `lowest-quote` below the foreach bank choice, are listed on no branch or only on the enclosing `when`, so a row locating them on another branch fails as misbound. The root declares `xmlns:ns0`; each `ns1` child of the root (`ns1:Episode`, `ns1:Bill `, `ns1:Subject`, `ns1:PatientId`) declares `xmlns:ns1` itself, and `ns1:PatientId` inside `ns1:Episode` declares nothing. DW-39's `as :date` reads the first 10 characters of the `startDate` and `endDate` text (all of it when shorter) as an ISO `yyyy-MM-dd` date and writes it as `yyyy-MM-dd`; `endDate` adds 5 days; a text that does not begin with an ISO date raises `DateTimeParseException`. Unqualified selectors take the first child element by local name in any namespace, and DW-41's `ns1#PatientId` also requires the model namespace. The DW 1.0 writer writes a null value as an inline-closed element and a string, the empty one included, between tags (0.6.1, as in D-113) | The namespace placement, the date coercion of the date-time text and the empty forms are read from the script and `reply.xml`, not observed on the runtime; Tier 2A decides the true bytes (D-029). | D-157 | Amends D-152: processing-orders-with-dataweave-and-APIkit-java `controller/RawBody.write(HttpServletResponse, int, byte[])` also sets the status, sets `Content-Length` to the body length, writes the bytes through the servlet output stream and flushes it, with no `Content-Type`, character encoding or locale (D-066); an empty array sends `Content-Length: 0` with no body. A `null` response or body throws `NullPointerException` before the response is touched. Its callers are `GlobalExceptionHandler.noListener`, the 404 `No listener for endpoint: <uri>` for a path outside the original listener path `/api/*` [processing-orders-with-dataweave-and-APIkit/src/main/app/currency.xml:33], and `GlobalExceptionHandler.unexpected`, the default-strategy 500; both reset the response before the call | Set no framing header and let Undertow choose (`Content-Length` when an unflushed body fits the buffer, `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` after an early flush); treat a `null` body as empty; set the status only when it differs from the current one | The 404 and 500 bodies are byte arrays, which Mule 3.8's listener frames with `Content-Length` (D-152, [raw.githubusercontent.com/mulesoft/mule/mule-3.8.0/modules/http/src/main/java/org/mule/module/http/internal/listener/HttpResponseBuilder.java:153-156,170-197,232-249]); both callers always pass a non-null array, and a `null` argument is a programming error that fails before any status or header is written; both callers reset the response first, so an unconditional `setStatus` sets the only status the response carries | A body longer than `Integer.MAX_VALUE` bytes cannot be framed by `setContentLength(int)`; the 404 message and the exception messages stay far below that; a header a future caller sets before the call without a reset is sent as set |
| D-158 | web-service-consumer-java's `exception/ReasonPhrase.set(String)` reads the request context with `ServletRequestContext.current()` and does nothing when there is none; with a context it hands the phrase unchanged to `HttpServerExchange.setReasonPhrase` [web-service-consumer-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/web_service_consumer/exception/ReasonPhrase.java:44-49]. No code in the project calls it: both listener flows set `statusCode="200"` and no `reasonPhrase`, and the file has no exception strategy [web-service-consumer/src/main/app/tshirt-service-consumer.xml:7-9,35-37], so every status line carries Undertow's standard phrase (D-010). Its Javadoc states that the phrase reaches the status line whether the status code is set before or after the call, and that a call after commit has no effect [ReasonPhrase.java:36-39] | `ServletRequestContext.requireCurrent()`, which throws `IllegalStateException` without a context, as salesforce-data-retrieval-java's copy does; leaving the class out of a project that sets no custom phrase; Javadoc stating that `HttpServerExchange.setStatusCode` resets the phrase and that the call must follow the status, as the file's plan worded it | AAP 0.4.1 places `exception/ReasonPhrase.java` in every project with an `http:listener` flow, and both flows of web-service-consumer are listener flows. Without a context (a MockMvc request or a plain unit test) a caller still writes its status and body, where `requireCurrent()` would throw. In Undertow 2.3.17.Final `HttpServerExchange.setStatusCode` changes only the status bits, `setReasonPhrase` stores an exchange attachment, and `HttpResponseConduit` writes that attachment, or `StatusCodes.getReason` when it is absent, at commit. Scratch runs on that version returned `HTTP/1.1 400 X` with the phrase set before `setStatus(400)` and after it, `HTTP/1.1 400 Invalid input data` for a phrase set before a handler returns `ResponseEntity.status(HttpStatus.BAD_REQUEST)`, `HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request` for a call after commit, after `set(null)` and from another thread, and `HTTP/1.1 200 OK` and `HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found` without a call; calls outside a request threw nothing | The class has no caller until one is added; a call on a thread without an Undertow request context, or after commit, has no effect and reports no error. An Undertow version that clears the phrase in `setStatusCode` makes a phrase set before the status disappear; a raw status-line test such as the `ReasonPhraseWireTest` of other projects detects that. | D-189 | Amends D-152: netsuite-data-retrieval-java `controller/RawBody` has two public methods that share one private writer. `write(HttpServletResponse, int, byte[])` sets the status, sets `Content-Length` to the body length, writes the bytes unchanged through the servlet output stream and flushes it; it sets no `Content-Type`, character encoding or locale (D-066). `writeJson(HttpServletResponse, int, byte[])` takes the same steps and also sets `Content-Type` to exactly `application/json`, with no `charset` parameter, before any byte is written. Callers: `GlobalExceptionHandler`'s five APIkit branches [netsuite-data-retrieval/src/main/app/netsuite-api.xml:107-133] and its three D-020 failure modes use `writeJson`; its default-strategy 500 (0.6.2) uses `write` | Return `ResponseEntity` with `MediaType.APPLICATION_JSON`; write through `getWriter()`; only `write`, with each caller setting `Content-Type` itself; no framing header (D-163); a `null` body raising `NullPointerException` (D-157) | Each `apikit:mapping` sets the property `Content-Type` to `application/json` and a literal payload [netsuite-data-retrieval/src/main/app/netsuite-api.xml:110-111,115-116,120-121,125-126,130-131], which Mule 3.8's listener sends as set (D-066), and the default 500 carries a string payload without `mimeType`, which it sends with no `Content-Type` (D-066). A scratch run on Boot 3.2.12 and Undertow 2.3.17 sent exactly `application/json` from `writeJson` for 400, 404, 405, 406 (request `Accept: text/plain`) and 429 (with the caller's `Retry-After`), and no `Content-Type` from `write` for 200 and 500; `setContentType("application/json")` followed by `getWriter()` sent `application/json;charset=ISO-8859-1`. A `ResponseEntity<byte[]>` with an explicit `application/json` type sent the same 406 header and body in that run; it would put the JSON branches and the header-less 500 on two write paths, while one private writer keeps the steps of both methods identical. The explicit length gives the `Content-Length` framing Mule's listener uses for a non-stream payload (D-152); the same run sent `Content-Length` without `Transfer-Encoding` for a 200 002-byte body. The 500 body is empty when the exception message is `null` | A body longer than `Integer.MAX_VALUE` bytes cannot be framed by `setContentLength(int)`; the APIkit, `ErrorResponse` and exception-message bodies stay far below that. A `Content-Type` set by a caller before `write` is sent as set |
