| D-194 | service-orchestration-and-choice-routing-java's `mapper/OrderXmlMapper` re-implements DW-32 and DW-33 [service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/main/app/mule-config.xml:9-16,27-34] on generic values with the DataWeave 1.0 selector, reader and writer rules, and keeps the `orderItems` shapes they derive [service-orchestration-and-choice-routing-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/service_orchestration_and_choice_routing/mapper/OrderXmlMapper.java]. `toOrderXml(String)` reads the page JSON with Jackson's streaming parser, duplicate names kept, numbers as their token text (a decimal through `BigDecimal.toPlainString()`) and booleans as `true`/`false`. `payload.orderItems.item` maps over the array and takes each object element's first `item` value, and the XML writer writes an array as its member name repeated once per element. The page's request `orderItems: [{"item": {...}}, ...]` therefore gives one `<orderItems>` element per item holding `manufacturer`, `name`, `productId` and `quantity` directly, with no `item` element; `[]` writes no `orderItems` element; a missing or `null` member writes `<orderItems/>`. The text starts with `<?xml version='1.0' encoding='UTF-8'?>`, indents two spaces per depth, joins lines with `\n`, ends without a newline, escapes `&`, `<` and `>` in text and writes member names unchanged. `toSummaryJson(String)` parses the `summary` with a namespace-aware JDK DOM parser (document type declarations refused, no external entity, DTD or XInclude, secure processing on), reads local names, every leaf as a string and repeated child elements as repeated members, and writes `{orderId, customer, orderItems}` as JSON indented two spaces with `": "`; a document element other than `summary` gives three `null` members. With the page's own request the JAXB `Order` (`@XmlElementWrapper(name = "orderItems")` around `item`) reads no item, the summary's `orderItems` is empty and DW-33 writes `"orderItems": ""`; a summary with items gives an object with one `"item"` member per item. The page's loop `i < orderSummary.orderItems.length` [service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/main/app/docroot/index.html:136-155] then never runs: the summary table stays empty while the second tab is still enabled and selected. Malformed JSON or XML, a JSON text without a value and content after the JSON value throw `IllegalArgumentException` with the parser's message; a `null` argument throws `NullPointerException`; an empty JSON object reads as `null`. Each `toSummaryJson` call configures its own `DocumentBuilderFactory.newDefaultInstance()`, and the builder's error handler is a `org.xml.sax.helpers.DefaultHandler`, which throws on a fatal parse error and prints nothing | Write `<orderItems><item>…</item></orderItems>` and answer an `orderItems` array of `{"item": …}` objects, the shapes the page reads and the AAP 0.3.8 `DocrootPageTest` sentence describes; bind the JSON and the summary to the `Order` and `OrderSummary` model classes; write the XML with StAX and the JSON with Jackson's `DefaultPrettyPrinter`; keep `{}` as an empty member list; reject member names that are not XML names; share one static `DocumentBuilderFactory`; keep the parser's default error handler | AAP 0.10.1 forbids altering transformation rules, 0.6.1 requires the DW 1.0 writer's rendering and D-043 keeps original defects. DW 1.0's single-value selector on an array maps over its elements, its XML writer renders an array value as repeated elements under the member name, and its XML reader turns `<orderItems><item>…</item></orderItems>` into an object and `<orderItems/>` into `""`. The scripts work on generic data, so no model class takes part. StAX `writeStartDocument` quotes the declaration with `"` and Jackson 2.15's default printer writes `" : "`, unlike the committed DW 1.0 outputs [get-customer-list-from-netsuite/src/test/resources/example.xml; processing-orders-with-dataweave-and-APIkit/src/main/resources/orders.json]. An empty object and `null` both select nothing and both write `<key/>`, so reading `{}` as `null` changes no output and leaves an empty list unambiguously an empty array. Neither script checks names, and a member name that is not an XML name makes the XSLT step that follows fail. JAXP does not require a `DocumentBuilderFactory` to be thread-safe, and the JDK parser's default error handler writes `[Fatal Error]` lines to standard error | On this project the page's order summary table stays empty after a successful order, unlike what the page's code and the AAP 0.3.8 sentence expect; `DocrootPageTest` and `OrderChannelControllerIntegrationTest` assert this derived outcome. The array-selector rule for elements that are not objects, the form written for an empty object and the error wording are derived, not observed: the Tier 2A fixtures `service-orchestration_order-request` and `service-orchestration_order-request-failure-reply` pin the bytes, and a mismatch is corrected in the mapper, never in a fixture (D-023). Nesting is read and written recursively, so a summary nested deeper than the call stack allows fails with `StackOverflowError`; the summary comes from the project's own `IProcessOrder` endpoint, whose model nests four levels |
| D-195 | Amends D-152: addition-using-javascript-transformer-java `controller/RawBody.write(HttpServletResponse, int, byte[])` sets the status, sets `Content-Length` to the body length, writes the bytes through the servlet output stream and flushes it, which commits the response; the stream is left open for the container. The order and the rendering of values the seed does not hold (non-ASCII text, a leading space, colon or `<`, lines over 76 columns, values that are neither `String` nor `byte[]`) are derived, not observed. The Tier 2A fixture `extracting-data-from-LDAP-directory_users-found` pins the bytes, and a mismatch is corrected in the mapper, never in a fixture (D-023). The Box flow's `rollback-exception-strategy` only logs `#[exception]` at INFO [:35-37], and the FIM flow has no strategy, so both answer the default 500 | The exact Mule 500 body is pinned only by the Tier 2A fixture `oauth2-client-credentials-using-the-HTTP-connector_oauth-error-500`; a difference it shows gets a new row, never a silent change |

A call on a thread without an Undertow request context, or after the response is committed, has no effect and reports no error; a custom phrase added later needs a raw status-line test such as the `ReasonPhraseWireTest` of other projects to detect a lost phrase. Callers: `BoxWebController.boxUserLoginFlow` (302 with the `Location` of [oauth2-authorization-code-using-the-HTTP-connector/src/main/app/http-authorization-code-web.xml:29-30] and the echoed request body), `BoxWebController.userLoginDoneFlow` (the 200 reply of :47), `BoxAuthorizationController.authorization` and `.redirectUrl` (the module-owned answers of :6-18), `PortPathGuardFilter` (the wrong-port 404, D-011) and `GlobalExceptionHandler` | `Content-Length` set to the body length, as D-152 does; `HttpServletResponse.sendRedirect` for the 302 answers; a `ResponseEntity` with a `Location` header; rethrow `IOException` as `UncheckedIOException`, as D-192 does | 0.4.2 states that `RawBody` sets no header, read literally here as in D-163. `sendRedirect` fixes the status at 302, clears the buffer and leaves the response committed, so it cannot carry the request body that `boxUserLoginFlow` echoes [oauth2-authorization-code-using-the-HTTP-connector/src/main/app/http-authorization-code-web.xml:27-35] or the module's non-302 answers; a `ResponseEntity` body passes Spring's converters, which add `text/plain` or `application/octet-stream` (D-066). Leaving the stream unopened for an empty body gives the `Content-Length: 0` that Mule 3.8's listener sends for a null payload (D-152). Every caller already declares `IOException` | A non-empty non-stream reply (`/web/loginDone`, the `/redirectUrl` 200) is chunked where Mule 3.8's listener sends `Content-Length` (D-152); HTTP/1.1 clients read the same bytes. | D-225 | importing-an-email-attachment-using-the-POP3-connector-java's `mapper/OrdersXmlMapper.toOrdersXml(byte[])` re-implements DW-14 [importing-an-email-attachment-using-the-POP3-connector/src/main/app/pop-to-xml.xml:13-25]. Reader: the first attachment's bytes are decoded as UTF-8 and parsed with commons-csv 1.14.1 `CSVFormat.DEFAULT` set to take the header from the first record and skip it, with `,` separator, `"` quote and `\` escape, empty lines ignored and values neither trimmed nor stripped of surrounding spaces; LF, CRLF and CR all end a record; the format is built with `CSVFormat.Builder.get()`, which returns the same format as the deprecated `Builder.build()`; header names match case-sensitively, other columns are ignored and the header's column order does not change the output order. Writer: Woodstox 6.5.1 `WstxOutputFactory`, instantiated directly, with `XMLOutputFactory2.P_AUTOMATIC_EMPTY_ELEMENTS` set to `false`. Layout: the declaration `<?xml version='1.0' encoding='UTF-8'?>` (single quotes), LF line feeds, two spaces of indentation per level and no trailing line feed; `orders` holds one `order` per data record with `orderId`, `name`, `units`, `pricePerUnit` in that order; input with no data record (zero bytes, a header only, or only empty lines) gives `<orders/>`. Field forms: a column the header lacks, or a cell missing from a record shorter than the header, gives `<x/>`; an empty cell gives `<x></x>`; any other value is written unchanged as text (`2.0` stays `2.0`) with Woodstox's escaping: `&` → `&amp;`, `<` → `&lt;`, `>` → `&gt;` only at the start of a value or after `]`, CR → `&#xd;`, quotes and tabs unchanged. Errors propagate unchecked to the poller, which logs them (0.6.2): `UncheckedIOException` for malformed CSV such as an unterminated quoted value, `IllegalArgumentException` for a header with an empty column name, `IllegalStateException` for a character XML 1.0 forbids. These are the POP3 forms: the IMAP project's DW-13 mapper is a separate copy (D-004) that takes a `String`, self-closes an empty value and escapes every `>` [importing-an-email-attachment-using-the-IMAP-connector/src/main/app/imap-to-xml.xml:17-29] [importing-an-email-attachment-using-the-POP3-connector-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/importing_an_email_attachment_using_the_pop3_connector/mapper/OrdersXmlMapper.java] | Hand-written text lines as in D-113; a DOM `Transformer` or Jackson `XmlMapper` serialiser; `XMLOutputFactory.newFactory()` provider lookup; Woodstox's default automatic empty elements; a double-quoted declaration or a trailing line feed; `<orders></orders>` for zero records; one form for missing and empty cells; RFC 4180 reading with no escape character; trimming values; the platform charset; the deprecated `CSVFormat.Builder.build()` | The committed DW 1.0 output `get-customer-list-from-netsuite/src/test/resources/example.xml` shows a single-quoted declaration, LF line feeds, two-space indentation and no trailing line feed (D-113), and Woodstox writes the declaration in that single-quoted form. DW 1.0 writes a null value (what `$.pricePerUnit` yields for a column the row lacks) as an inline-closed empty element, writes a string, the empty one included, between start and end tags, and writes an object with no fields (`orders: {}` after `payload map` over no rows) as an empty element; null handling follows the DW 1.0 writer (0.6.1), and the forms match DW-04's (D-113). The DataWeave CSV reader's documented defaults are `,` separator, `"` quote, `\` escape, a header row and empty lines ignored, and it represents every value as a string (D-177). The flow sets no encoding, and Mule 3's default `mule.encoding` is UTF-8. Direct instantiation fixes the StAX provider: `newFactory()` returns whichever implementation the classpath lists first, and the JDK's built-in writer quotes the declaration with `"`; with automatic empty elements on, Woodstox writes an empty cell as `<x/>`. commons-csv 1.14.1 marks `Builder.build()` deprecated and `get()` not | The layout and field forms are read from DW 1.0's documented behaviour and from DW-04's committed output, not observed on DW-14; the Tier 2A `importing-an-email-attachment-using-the-POP3-connector_one-attachment` and `_two-attachments-first-used` captures confirm them, and a fixture showing other bytes overrides this row through a new row. The `>` and CR escapes are Woodstox's and undocumented for DW 1.0. The IMAP twin writes an empty cell as `<x/>` and this mapper as `<x></x>`, so the two projects differ for that input until the captures settle it. A byte-order mark, duplicate header names and attachments in another charset are unverified against the original |

A missing or blank `redirect_uri` is accepted, and Spring Authorization Server then redirects to the registered `http://localhost:<http.listener.port>/redirect`. | D-250 | Amends D-239: scatter-gather-flow-control-java's `exception/ReasonPhrase` has two public methods. `set(HttpServletResponse, String)` reads the request context with `ServletRequestContext.current()` and, when there is one and the given response reports `isCommitted()` as `false`, hands the phrase unchanged to `HttpServerExchange.setReasonPhrase`. `set(String)` passes the context's `getOriginalResponse()`, Undertow's `HttpServletResponseImpl`, to the first method. Neither changes the status code, a header or the body; both return without effect, with no exception and no log entry, on a thread without an Undertow request context or for a committed response, and a `null` phrase clears a phrase set earlier [scatter-gather-flow-control-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/scatter_gather_flow_control/exception/ReasonPhrase.java:52-75]. Its caller is `GlobalExceptionHandler.noListener`, which answers a path with no handler by setting 404, then the phrase `Not Found`, then writing the body; `GlobalExceptionHandler.unexpected` sets no phrase, so its 500 carries Undertow's standard `Internal Server Error`. The original flow `scatter-gatherFlow` has no response builder and no exception strategy [scatter-gather-flow-control/src/main/app/scatter-gather.xml:6-60] (D-010) | `set(HttpServletResponse, String)` alone, as planned for this project's handler; `set(String)` alone, as in the copies of D-128, D-158, D-160, D-168 and D-190; unwrapping each `ServletResponseWrapper` down to `HttpServletResponseImpl`, as login-form-using-the-http-connector-java's copy does (D-239); `ServletRequestContext.requireCurrent()`, which throws `IllegalStateException` without a context (D-182) | The 0.3.2 mapping names the helper `ReasonPhrase.set(String)`, and this project's handler passes the `HttpServletResponse` it receives, so both forms exist; the one-argument form routes through the response form, which leaves one code path to the exchange. Overloads share the identity `ReasonPhrase#set`, so one backward row covers both (D-010). The thread-bound context reaches the exchange whatever wrappers surround the response, so no unwrapping is needed on the request thread. Without a context (MockMvc, a plain unit test) the handler still writes its status and body, where `requireCurrent()` would throw. On Undertow 2.3.17.Final `HttpServerExchange.setStatusCode` and `HttpServletResponseImpl.setStatus` leave the phrase attachment unchanged (bytecode), so the documented order of status, phrase and body is a usage contract rather than a constraint. A scratch run of the module on Undertow 2.3.17.Final read the raw status lines `HTTP/1.1 400 Invalid input data` through the response form, also from an `@ExceptionHandler`; `HTTP/1.1 400 Missing input data` through the one-argument form; `HTTP/1.1 400 Wrapped phrase` through two nested `HttpServletResponseWrapper`s; `HTTP/1.1 409 Early phrase` for a phrase set before `setStatus(409)`; `HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request` for a call after commit, a call on another thread and a `null` phrase; and `HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found` with the body `No listener for endpoint: /unknown?x=1` and no `Content-Type` for an advice in `noListener`'s order. MockMvc and plain unit calls threw nothing | `Not Found` is also Undertow's standard phrase for 404, so a lost call does not show on the wire for this project; a call without an Undertow request context, on another thread or after commit has no effect and reports no error. `set(String)` has no caller in the project. A `null` response throws `NullPointerException` when a request context exists. Relies on Undertow internals, as D-010 does |
| D-251 | proxying-a-soap-api-java's `mapper/WsdlAddressMapper.rewriteAddress`, which `SoapProxyService` calls for `GET /?wsdl` (D-059), parses the upstream WSDL with JAXP DOM, namespace-aware, with `FEATURE_SECURE_PROCESSING` on, `disallow-doctype-decl` on, external general and parameter entities off, XInclude off and entity references unexpanded [proxying-a-soap-api-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/proxying_a_soap_api/mapper/WsdlAddressMapper.java:134-146]. A WSDL that carries a document type declaration, or is not well-formed, raises `IllegalStateException` with the parser exception as its cause. The method sets only the `location` of the first `soap:address` or `soap12:address` child of `wsdl:definitions[@targetNamespace]/wsdl:service[@name]/wsdl:port[@name]`, matched by namespace URI and local name over direct children with the `cxf.proxy-service` namespace, service and port [proxying-a-soap-api/src/main/app/soap-api-proxy.xml:7]; when a step does not match it returns the upstream array unchanged. After a rewrite it writes the JDK identity transform of a copy of the document that declares no encoding, with the same XML version and standalone flag, so the bytes are UTF-8 for every upstream encoding [WsdlAddressMapper.java:223-249]. No `xsd:import`, `xsd:include` or `wsdl:import` location is rewritten and no `?xsd=` or `?wsdl=` resource is served | Allow a document type declaration and only block external DTD and entity loading; a text or regex rewrite of the `location` value; rewrite the address of every port; transform the parsed document directly, or write it with `LSSerializer` and an `LSOutput` encoding of UTF-8; rewrite import locations to `?xsd=`/`?wsdl=` URLs and serve them, as CXF does for a served WSDL (0.3.2) | The WSDL is fetched from a remote host, and a document type declaration carries both internal entity expansion and external entity references; refusing it closes both without a DTD processing path, and WSDL 1.1 documents are described by XML Schemas, not by a DTD. A namespace-aware DOM match finds the address whatever prefixes the upstream document uses, which a text rewrite does not. Rewriting one port follows the one `service` and `port` the `cxf:proxy-service` names. Returning the input array when nothing matches keeps the upstream bytes exact (D-059). A scratch run on JDK 17.0.20.1 showed that the identity transform of a parsed `Document` writes the encoding that document declares and ignores `OutputKeys.ENCODING`: an ISO-8859-1 input came out as ISO-8859-1 bytes and a UTF-16 input as UTF-16 with a byte-order mark, while `SoapProxyService` labels the reply `text/xml; charset=UTF-8`. The copy keeps the identity transform and changes only the encoding written: for a UTF-8 input its output equals the direct transform byte for byte. 0.4.2 and D-059 name only the address for this project, and the upstream WSDL could not be read while the project was written (`www.predic8.com:8080` did not answer), so whether it imports anything is unknown | An upstream WSDL with a document type declaration, which CXF would have served, answers 500 here. After a rewrite the XML declaration reads `<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" standalone="no"?>` (no `standalone` for a document declared standalone), whitespace outside the document element is dropped and character references are written as characters or predefined entities, so the bytes differ from the upstream copy beyond the attribute while the infoset does not. If the upstream WSDL imports schemas or WSDLs by relative location, a client resolves them against the proxy URL, where nothing serves them; absolute upstream locations still resolve upstream |
| D-252 | service-orchestration-and-choice-routing-java's `config/JtaConfig` carries the `jbossts:transaction-manager` properties [service-orchestration-and-choice-routing/src/main/app/fulfillment.xml:12-15] onto the objects of the Narayana starter, whose `JtaTransactionManager` bean `transactionManager` is the only `PlatformTransactionManager`, shared by the JMS listeners and the Derby JDBC work (D-026). `defaultTimeout` 600 maps to `JtaTransactionManager.setDefaultTimeout` (seconds), applied by the bean post-processor `JtaConfig.JtaDefaultTimeoutApplier` from `transaction-manager.default-timeout`. `txReaperTimeout` 1000000 maps to Arjuna's `CoordinatorEnvironmentBean.txReaperTimeout` (milliseconds), applied by the bean factory post-processor `JtaConfig.ArjunaReaperTimeoutInitializer` (`PriorityOrdered`, `HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE`) from `transaction-manager.tx-reaper-timeout`. Both post-processors read their key from the `Environment`; both static `@Bean` methods declare the nested concrete type; a reaper timeout of 0 or less fails startup with `IllegalStateException` [service-orchestration-and-choice-routing-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/service_orchestration_and_choice_routing/config/JtaConfig.java] | Declare an own `JtaTransactionManager` or JTA `TransactionManager` bean; Boot's `spring.transaction.default-timeout` through `TransactionManagerCustomizers`; `narayana.default-timeout` alone; a `jbossts-properties.xml` file or the `com.arjuna.ats.arjuna.coordinator.txReaperTimeout` system property; a `config/*Properties` record; `@Bean` methods typed as `BeanFactoryPostProcessor` and `BeanPostProcessor` | A declared manager makes the starter's class-level `@ConditionalOnMissingBean(PlatformTransactionManager.class)` back off. narayana-spring-boot 3.2.0 has no reaper-timeout property (`NarayanaPropertiesInitializer` sets `defaultTimeout`, the object store and the recovery settings only), and `TransactionReaper.instantiate()` reads `txReaperTimeout` once, on the first transaction; the value is therefore set during bean factory post-processing, before any singleton exists and before `@Value` or `@ConfigurationProperties` binding is available. Spring files a bean factory post-processor under `PriorityOrdered` by the declared return type of its factory method: in a scratch context an interface-typed `HIGHEST_PRECEDENCE` post-processor ran after a concrete `PriorityOrdered` one of order 0. A system property or a properties file would move the value out of `application.yml` (0.3.5). In a full `@SpringBootTest` the context held one manager, `transactionManager`, with default timeout 600 and reaper timeout 1000000, and `DataSourceTransactionManagerAutoConfiguration` backed off | `CoordinatorEnvironmentBean` is JVM-wide: the reaper timeout applies to the whole process, and a reaper started earlier in the same JVM, for example by another test context, keeps its earlier period. The default timeout is held in two settings derived from one key: the JTA manager's default (this row) and Arjuna's coordinator default (`narayana.default-timeout`) |
| D-253 | proxying-a-soap-api-java's `exception/ReasonPhrase.set(String)` hands the phrase unchanged to `HttpServerExchange.setReasonPhrase` through `ServletRequestContext.requireCurrent()`, which throws `IllegalStateException` on a thread without an Undertow servlet request [proxying-a-soap-api-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/proxying_a_soap_api/exception/ReasonPhrase.java:42-46]; this is the form of D-182 and D-209. Its public surface is `set(String)` alone, and callers pass the phrase only, with no request argument. Its caller is `GlobalExceptionHandler.noListener`, which sets status 404, then the phrase `Not Found`, then writes the body `No listener for endpoint: <path>` for a path other than `/` on the listener of flow `main` [proxying-a-soap-api/src/main/app/soap-api-proxy.xml:6] (D-010). Its Javadoc states that the phrase applies when set after the status and before the response is committed, and does not state that `HttpServerExchange.setStatusCode` clears the phrase | `set(HttpServletRequest, String)` with the request parameter unused, as the file's plan worded it; `ServletRequestContext.current()` with no effect when there is no context, as the copies of D-128, D-158, D-160, D-168, D-190 and D-242 do; Javadoc stating that `setStatusCode` resets a phrase set earlier, as the file's plan worded it | The 0.3.2 mapping names the helper `ReasonPhrase.set(String)`, and every other project's copy has that signature, so the exchange is reached through the Undertow context bound to the request thread and no unused parameter is added. The original sets no custom reason phrase: its listener has no response builder and flow `main` has no exception strategy [proxying-a-soap-api/src/main/app/soap-api-proxy.xml:5-12], and `Not Found` is Undertow's standard phrase for 404, so the 404 status line is the same with or without the call. The file's plan names `requireCurrent()`, and the project's endpoint tests run on Undertow through `@SpringBootTest(webEnvironment = RANDOM_PORT)` (D-057), where every request thread carries a request context; a call outside an Undertow request fails with an error instead of leaving the standard phrase without notice. In Undertow 2.3.17.Final `HttpServerExchange.setStatusCode` changes only the status bits, `HttpServletResponseImpl.setStatus` only calls it, and `setReasonPhrase` stores an exchange attachment (bytecode), so the plan's reset statement does not hold for the pinned version. A scratch run of this file on Undertow 2.3.17.Final over a raw socket returned `HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found` with no `Content-Type` and the body `No listener for endpoint: /other` for `GET /other` and `GET /other?x=1` in `noListener`'s call order, `HTTP/1.1 400 Invalid input data` with the phrase set after `setStatus(400)`, `HTTP/1.1 418 Before Status` with it set before `setStatus(418)`, `HTTP/1.1 201 Created Phrase` before a `ResponseEntity.status(201)` return, `HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request` after `set(null)`, and `HTTP/1.1 200 OK` for a call after commit; calls outside a request and from another thread threw `IllegalStateException` | A call under MockMvc or in a plain unit test throws `IllegalStateException`; the 404 branch needs a raw status-line test on `RANDOM_PORT`. An Undertow version that clears the phrase in `setStatusCode` makes a phrase set before the status disappear. Relies on Undertow internals, as D-010 does |

| D-257 | querying-a-mysql-database-java's `exception/ReasonPhrase` has two public methods. `set(String)` hands the phrase unchanged to `HttpServerExchange.setReasonPhrase` through `ServletRequestContext.requireCurrent()`, which throws `IllegalStateException` (`UT000048: No request is currently active`) on a thread without an Undertow servlet request. `set(HttpServletResponse, String)` rejects a `null` response with `NullPointerException` and a committed response with `IllegalStateException`, then calls `set(String)`. The response form checks the one precondition it can read from the response: a phrase stored after commit never reaches the status line, and the call then fails with an error instead of leaving the standard phrase without notice, as `requireCurrent()` does off-request. The original listener binds only `/` and sets no reason phrase [querying-a-mysql-database/src/main/app/database-to-json.xml:6], so `Not Found` is the only phrase the project writes. On Undertow 2.3.17.Final `HttpServerExchange.setStatusCode` and `HttpServletResponseImpl.setStatus` change only the status bits and leave the `REASON_PHRASE` attachment unchanged (bytecode), so the phrase survives a later status change; the documented call order is status, then phrase, then body. A call under MockMvc or in a plain unit test throws `IllegalStateException`, so tests that reach `noListener` run on `@SpringBootTest(webEnvironment = RANDOM_PORT)`. The project carries a public `set(String)` that none of its code calls. Relies on Undertow internals, as D-010 does |
| D-258 | processing-orders-with-dataweave-and-APIkit-java `service/CurrencyService.getCurrencies()` reads `classpath:currency.json` through the injected `ResourceLoader` on its first call, keeps the bytes in a `volatile` field and returns a new copy of them on every call; the constructor only stores the loader. An attribute spelled other than exactly `create=true` (for example `create=TRUE`) stays in the connection attributes and Derby reads it there. The Derby engine stays booted until the JVM exits |

| D-270 | upload-to-ftp-after-converting-json-to-xml-java's `mapper/EmployeesXmlMapper.toXml(byte[])` re-implements DW-34 of flow `main` [upload-to-ftp-after-converting-json-to-xml/src/main/app/upload-to-ftp.xml:9-26] by hand. Jackson's streaming `JsonParser` (a `JsonFactory` with default features and default stream-read constraints) reads the body into an ordered model that keeps duplicate keys and each number's JSON text; a `StringBuilder` writes the XML. Layout: the declaration `<?xml version='1.0' encoding='UTF-8'?>` (single quotes), one element per line, one tab per nesting level, LF line ends and no LF after the last line, UTF-8 bytes. Selection: a key is read from its first occurrence and a JSON `null` counts as absent; an `employees` value that is not an object counts as absent; each `employee` holds `name`, `lastName` and `addresses` in that order whatever the input order; an `employee` item that is not an object gives `<name/>`, `<lastName/>` and `<addresses/>`; an `addresses` value that is not an object gives `<addresses/>`; each `address` item is written as it stands, every field in input order, duplicates included. Values: `null` and an absent `name` or `lastName` give `<k/>`, the empty string `<k></k>`, an object none of whose fields writes an element (`{}` included) `<k/>`; an array writes each item under the same key at the same level, so nested arrays flatten and `[]` writes nothing; numbers keep their JSON text (`1.10`, `1e3`), booleans give `true`/`false`; text escapes `&`, `<` and `>` only; JSON keys are element names verbatim. `IllegalArgumentException`, which reaches the default-strategy 500 (0.6.2), is raised for a `null` array, zero bytes or whitespace only (`Request body is empty`), malformed JSON or a violated stream-read constraint (`Request body is not valid JSON`, parser exception as cause), a second root value (`Unexpected content after the JSON root value`), a root that is not an object (`JSON root is not an object`) and a present `employee` or `address` value that is not an array (`employees.employee is not an array`, `addresses.address is not an array`). The committed `reply.xml` shows that writer's layout for the sample: single-quoted declaration, one element per line, tab indentation [upload-to-ftp-after-converting-json-to-xml/src/test/resources/reply.xml]. The original test compares with every whitespace character removed [upload-to-ftp-after-converting-json-to-xml/src/test/java/org/mule/examples/UploadToFtpAfterConvertingJsonToXmlIT.java:98], so the fixture's final LF and its `123 MainStreet` spacing pin nothing; the last line ends without LF, as in the other hand-written DW 1.0 XML writers (D-113, D-216). Hand-written text fixes every byte, where serialisers choose their own declaration quotes, indentation and empty-element forms, and `jackson-dataformat-xml` is not on this project's classpath. The streaming parser keeps what a `JsonNode` tree drops or rewrites: duplicate keys and number text such as `1.10`. The script's object constructor fixes the `name`, `lastName`, `addresses` order; `address: $` carries the item unchanged; `<k/>` for `null`, `<k></k>` for the empty string and one element per array item are the DW 1.0 writer forms D-113 applies. A `null` array and zero bytes both mean no body. The error messages name the failing input, and an `IllegalArgumentException` keeps the flow's absent exception strategy, the HTTP default 500 | These rules are inferred from the script and the DW 1.0 writer, not captured from a Mule run; `EmployeesXmlMapperTest` pins each. | D-294 | netsuite-data-retrieval-java's `mapper/NetsuiteValueMapper.writeRecord` applies the 0.6.5 output rules and settles the REST inputs those rules leave open as follows [netsuite-data-retrieval-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/netsuite_data_retrieval/mapper/NetsuiteValueMapper.java]. A top-level key, and a member of an address subrecord, is matched to the SOAP name exactly first and then ignoring case. STRING takes text unchanged, a number or boolean as its text, and an object as its textual `refName`. BOOLEAN and the Address `override` take a boolean or the text `true`/`false` in any case. A DATE_TIME value that is not text, or is neither an offset datetime nor a full-date, is omitted. `stage` and `creditHoldOverride` compare only a textual `id`, `refName` or value with the candidates. The Address text members take text or a number; `country` takes a textual code or an object's `id`, and a code `java.util.Locale` does not know is written as the code itself, upper-cased; `state` takes a textual `state`, else `dropdownstate.id`. Taking each scalar form that already carries the value keeps a field REST does return, while a value no rule can render is still omitted and never invented. A `null` RecordRef member keeps the member set of the examples, so the 0.6.5 acceptance rules fail that element in `NetsuiteApiIT` rather than the key vanishing. Exact-first lookup puts an exact REST key ahead of a case variant. | D-300 | Amends D-036: salesforce-to-MySQL-DB-using-Batch-Processing-java's `service/FileWatermarkStore` keeps each key in its own UTF-8 file `<watermark.store-dir>/<key>`, so the watermark lives in `<store-dir>/timestamp`. `read(key)` reads the file on every call, strips leading and trailing whitespace, and returns `Optional.empty()` when the file is absent, not a regular file, or blank. `write(key, value)` creates the directory, writes exactly `value` (no trailing newline) to a temporary `<key>-<n>.tmp` in the same directory, and moves it over the target with `ATOMIC_MOVE` and `REPLACE_EXISTING`, repeating the move with `REPLACE_EXISTING` alone on `AtomicMoveNotSupportedException`. Every I/O failure is thrown as `UncheckedIOException` naming the file, after the temporary file is deleted (a failed deletion is attached as suppressed). The store does not cache, log, or hold a default; `SalesforceToDatabaseBatchJob.triggerFlow` decides when it reads and writes [salesforce-to-MySQL-DB-using-Batch-Processing-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/salesforce_to_mysql_db_using_batch_processing/service/FileWatermarkStore.java] | One `java.util.Properties` file for all keys; writing the target in place with `Files.writeString`; an in-memory cache in front of the file; returning the raw content unstripped; checked `IOException` in the method signatures; logging inside the store | Mule 3.8.0 keeps the watermark as one entry per key in its persistent user object store (0.3.6), and a file per key is the direct equivalent with no format of its own. Writing to a temporary file and renaming it means an interrupted write leaves the previous complete value in place, so the next poll never reads a truncated watermark. Reading on every call reflects the file as it stands after a restart or a hand edit, and stripping tolerates the trailing newline an editor adds. An unchecked exception reaches `triggerFlow` like any other poll failure, which logs it and leaves the watermark unchanged (0.6.2); the caller owns that log line | On a file system that refuses atomic moves, the fallback move is not atomic, and a concurrent reader could observe the replacement in progress; the only caller is the single scheduler thread. A `*.tmp` file remains if the JVM stops between creating and moving it, or if its deletion fails; it is never read. The directory must be writable (D-036) |

| D-303 | Amends D-108: http-request-response-with-logger-java keeps `config/DispatcherServletConfig` and its nested `AllMethodsDispatcherServlet` [http-request-response-with-logger-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/http_request_response_with_logger/config/DispatcherServletConfig.java:47-57,71-152], although Boot's own `DispatcherServlet` puts the same bytes on the wire once `RawBody.write` has committed the response (D-222). The `dispatcherServlet` bean applies the five `spring.mvc` settings that Boot applies to its auto-configured servlet: `dispatch-options-request`, `dispatch-trace-request`, `throw-exception-if-no-handler-found`, `publish-request-handled-events` and `log-request-details`. The subclass keeps the two dispatch flags in fields without initializers [same file:81,87], so a new instance holds `true` for OPTIONS, the value the `DispatcherServlet` constructor passes to the overridden setter, and `false` for TRACE. No Undertow deployment customizer is added for TRACE | Remove the class and rely on `RawBody.write` committing the response before `FrameworkServlet` adds its `Allow` header or `message/http` echo; keep the class and also add an `UndertowDeploymentInfoCustomizer` that clears the disallowed request methods | A scratch copy of the project, with a catch-all `EchoController` built to its plan (`echo` on `/**` and `echoOptions` for OPTIONS), ran on Boot 3.2.12 and Undertow 2.3.17.Final. With the class, `GET /echo`, `OPTIONS /moon`, `TRACE /x`, `PATCH /p`, `PUT`, `DELETE` and `POST` each answered 200 with the path as body, `Content-Length` and no `Content-Type`, `Allow` or `message/http`, and `HEAD /h` answered 200 with `Content-Length: 2` and no body. Without the class, `OPTIONS /moon` and `TRACE /x` received the same responses. In that run `FrameworkServlet.doOptions` and `doTrace` still called `HttpServlet.doOptions` and `doTrace` after the handler had committed the response, and the committed response discarded their `Allow` header and echo; DEBUG logging of `io.undertow` and `org.springframework.web` showed no error. D-108's scratch results, taken with a handler that does not commit, show that header and echo reaching the client. With the class, OPTIONS and TRACE end in `processRequest`, so no framework write follows the handler's response, whatever the handler or the container does with a committed response. Unit runs of the subclass on mock requests confirmed both branches: with dispatching disabled, `super.doOptions` added an `Allow` header that lists `PATCH` and `super.doTrace` wrote a `message/http` echo; with it enabled, the handler's body was the whole response. A newly constructed instance held `true` and `false`. Undertow 2.3.17.Final's `DeploymentInfo` has no disallowed-methods member (`javap`), and TRACE reached the handler, so the customizer would change nothing | The class has no effect a client can see while every handler of the project commits its response, so a wire-level test of OPTIONS or TRACE, such as `EchoControllerIntegrationTest`'s, passes with or without it, and only its unit tests and the context check that `dispatcherServlet` is an `AllMethodsDispatcherServlet` detect its removal. As D-108 states, a `spring.mvc` setting that a later Boot version applies to its own servlet is not applied here until `DispatcherServletConfig` applies it, and a Spring Framework change to `doOptions`, `doTrace` or `processRequest` needs the class revisited |

Without validation the context starts with the committed `TODO` placeholders of D-012 and nothing connects at startup | `****` also appears when the password is unset, so `toString()` cannot show a missing password; blank or padded values pass through unchanged and fail only on the first `POST /` (for example `https://TODO/ccx/service/TODO/Revenue_Management/v35.0`) |
| D-305 | extracting-data-from-LDAP-directory-java's `config/LdapConfig` replaces `ldap:config` and its `ldap:connection-pooling-profile` [extracting-data-from-LDAP-directory/src/main/app/ldap.xml:3-5] with two beans of its own: an `LdapContextSource` and an `LdapTemplate` over it. Both are bound from the `ldap.*` keys through the record `LdapConfig.LdapProperties(dn, password, port, url, search)`, whose nested `Search(baseDn, filter)` binds `ldap.search.base-dn` and `ldap.search.filter` [extracting-data-from-LDAP-directory/src/main/app/ldap.xml:9]. `ldap.url` is split with `String` operations at the first `/` after `://`: the text before it is the server URL, the text after it the context-source base (`ldap://localhost:10389/dc=my-domain,dc=com` gives `ldap://localhost:10389` and `dc=my-domain,dc=com`; a value without that `/`, such as the committed `TODO`, is the whole server URL with an empty base). Entry DNs therefore come back relative to `dc=my-domain,dc=com`, for example `cn=mmc,ou=people`, as the original test asserts [extracting-data-from-LDAP-directory/src/test/java/org/mule/examples/ExtractingDataFromLdapDirectoryIT.java:113-115]. The pooling profile (`exhaustedAction="WHEN_EXHAUSTED_GROW"`, `initialisationPolicy="INITIALISE_ONE"`) becomes `setPooled(true)`: the JDK LDAP provider's connection pool at its defaults, one connection created first per connection identity and no maximum size. `ldap.port` binds as a `String`, and no bean reads it directly. The bean methods open no connection, so the context starts with the committed TODO values (D-012). Spring Boot's `LdapAutoConfiguration` context source and template back off (`@ConditionalOnMissingBean`); no auto-configuration is excluded and no `spring.ldap.*` key is read. An unset `ldap.url` fails startup with `IllegalStateException`. Both records keep their generated `toString` [extracting-data-from-LDAP-directory-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/extracting_data_from_ldap_directory/config/LdapConfig.java] | Spring Boot's `spring.ldap.urls`, `base`, `username` and `password` keys with the auto-configured beans; the whole `ldap.url` as the server URL with no base; parsing `ldap.url` with `java.net.URI` or `java.net.URL`; Spring LDAP's `PoolingContextSource` over commons-pool2 with an unbounded `WHEN_EXHAUSTED_GROW` pool; no pooling; validating the connection at startup; a record `toString` that masks the password | The original key names stay (0.3.5, D-012), and Boot's keys have no Mule counterpart. The Mule `url` carries the base DN, and the original's entries are named relative to it, which a context-source base reproduces for every search. `java.net.URL` has no `ldap` handler (`MalformedURLException: unknown protocol: ldap`), and `java.net.URI` gives no host for a non-numeric port such as `localhost:TODO` (`parseServerAuthority` fails with `Illegal character in port number`), while string splitting accepts any configured text. The JDK pool's defaults (`com.sun.jndi.ldap.connect.pool.initsize` 1, no `maxsize`) mirror `INITIALISE_ONE` and `WHEN_EXHAUSTED_GROW` and need no further library (D-063). `mvn clean install` and the application must start without an LDAP server or credentials (0.7.1), which startup validation would prevent | Pool size and timeouts are JVM-wide `com.sun.jndi.ldap.connect.pool.*` system properties, not per-application settings, and idle pooled connections have no timeout by default. The generated `toString` of `LdapProperties` includes `ldap.password`; no class logs or prints the record |
| D-306 | import-leads-into-salesforce-java carries no counterpart of `sfdc:connection-pooling-profile exhaustedAction="WHEN_EXHAUSTED_GROW" initialisationPolicy="INITIALISE_ONE"` [import-leads-into-salesforce/src/main/app/import-leads-into-salesforce.xml:5]. `config/SalesforceProperties` binds only the six keys `sfdc.user`, `sfdc.password`, `sfdc.securityToken`, `sfdc.key`, `sfdc.secret` and `sfdc.login-url`, and no pool key [import-leads-into-salesforce-java/src/main/java/com/mulesoft/examples/import_leads_into_salesforce/config/SalesforceProperties.java]. `client/SalesforceSessionProvider` creates one `PartnerConnection` on the first Salesforce call, caches it for every later `query` and `create` call of `CreateLeadsBatchJob`, and replaces it only through the single re-authentication of D-020 | Keys `sfdc.connection-pooling-profile.exhausted-action` and `.initialisation-policy` that no code reads; a pool of `PartnerConnection`s that grows on demand and opens one connection at startup | The profile holds literals only, no `${...}` placeholder, and configures the Mule connector's own instance pool, which has no Java counterpart. The profile sets no `dynamics.service-url`, `dynamics.oauth.token-uri`, `file.inbound-endpoint.path` or `file.inbound-endpoint.move-to-directory`: a `test`-profile class that reaches Dataverse or the poller binds them through `@DynamicPropertySource` to its MockWebServer URLs and JUnit temporary directories (D-039), and may lower `dynamics.response-timeout` there as well. `spring.main.keep-alive` and the two timeouts configure the test JVM and its connections to the in-JVM stubs rather than a test actor, so, like D-275, this row amends the literal reading of 0.3.5 that the test profile holds values for in-JVM test actors only | Inherit the `application.yml` values `connect-timeout: 30000`, `response-timeout: 60000` and `keep-alive: true`; keep the `TODO` OAuth placeholders in tests; fixed `http://localhost:<port>` stub URLs and `target/` directories in the profile, as D-275 does for file paths; a `@MockBean` of the Dataverse client in every `@SpringBootTest` | With the `application.yml` timeouts, a test whose MockWebServer withholds its reply (the D-020 timeout case) or whose connection attempt hangs waits up to 60 s or 30 s; 1000 ms bounds that wait to one second, and both values stay positive, so `DynamicsOAuth2Config` builds the same connector as in production. With `keep-alive: true`, Spring Boot 3.2.12 starts a non-daemon `keep-alive` thread on every context refresh and stops it only on context close (`SpringApplication$KeepAlive`); `false` starts none, so no such thread lives in the surefire JVM while cached test contexts stay open. MockWebServer ports and JUnit temporary directories are chosen per class at run time, which a fixed profile value cannot express, so concurrent builds share no port or directory and no test writes under `src/main/resources` (D-039). A MockWebServer reply slower than 1000 ms on a loaded build host counts as a timeout. Tier 1 does not exercise the production timeouts 30000 and 60000 |

## Adding rows

- A new row is appended to the end of the table above with the next free ID (the first execution-time row was D-082) and fills all five cells.
- The requester's approval of D-016, D-027, D-032 or D-078 is recorded as a new row. Only such a row changes the affected project's Delivery label from `BLOCKED — pending approval of <D-ID>` to `DELIVERED` (D-079). An approval of D-016 that accepts named FB-NS items as deviations lists those items in its row.
- These results are also recorded as new rows: each `volatile` regex of a Tier 2A fixture (0.7.2); the NetSuite field-coverage and SuiteQL name results (FB-NS-02, FB-NS-09); the status and body the RAML 409 diagnostic capture of rest-api-with-apikit observes (0.3.3, D-008); a revised Workday WSDL hash (D-078); and the exact version of a supplied `sapjco3.jar` patch (D-047).
- A project README and its `TRACEABILITY.md` section cite the ID of every row that concerns the project.
